## Outcome (read this first)

Four windows. First window (five commits, `9aa60588`..`d320732c`): VM perf increments plus
the typed-storage flip. Second window (Dev: "continue for four hours", six more commits
`9164f05b`..`9c4326ab`): the representation rule extended to every primitive element type,
then differential-fuzz bands over the new kinds (5,660 programs, zero crashes), which found

Third window (Dev: "Let's continue on this", seven code commits `c4afb525`..`c5a47278`,
rows #13–#18): more fuzz bands (13,000 further programs, zero crashes) that found and fixed
an interpreter typed-declaration raise (#13) and an interpreter **budget hole** (#14, a loop
shape that ran unbudgeted forever), then the **object kind** (#15, `array<UserType>` as a
slot buffer), the **boundary one-class-check** (#16, 4.5× on `array<K>` →
`std::vector<std::shared_ptr<K>>`, plus a use-after-free in conversion error text), the
**slot-to-slot instance clone** (#17) and the **O(1) instance unregister** (#18) — together
10.7× on object-array copies and −42% / −50% on the by-value BST row. Fourth window (Dev
answered the three rulings, rows #21–#23): `++`/`--` on every lvalue (a parse-time desugar
to `+= 1` with an exact postfix old value; `.jaibite` version 8), map key types enforced on
direct stores with the array's `auto` inference rule applied to maps, and class-typed
arrays admitting subclasses (exact id first, a parent walk only on a name mismatch; the
object slot's per-element tag made it a rule change, not a layout change).
Fifth window (Dev: "resume our call floor", with the standing rule that register-file / call-floor
work must not break operator overloading; row #24): the function-call machinery measured at the
profile floor, so the increment went where the profile pointed — the METHOD path: the dead
this-field cache re-armed, the method IC's shadow guard memoized, overload resolution without the
per-call atomic pair, and (Dev's mid-window note) null compares on either side plus object
truthiness on storage-only lanes. Then instance creation (#25: lazy init env, pooled carrier, arena field cells, make_shared — a two-field construction 1.61 → 0.90 µs) and the write-side field cache with churn-proof field caches (#26: field stores ~45 → 10 ns, entity_tick −16%, Doom 5.80 → 5.68 ms/tic). Method calls 104 → 78 ns, the 20k-node BST −42% end to end, GLOOM 2.315 → 2.29,
operator overloads pinned (a class `operator==` is never consulted for a null operand; a global
`==` overload latches the gate and the lane steps aside). Row #27 (workflow-built) landed parse-time type proofs: the checker's walk runs in every mode and stamps same-type/field proofs both backends consume behind total guards (proofs on/off byte-identical; 5,842 generator programs + 193 adversarial attacks found none) - perf-neutral in v1, the enabler for handle/container proofs and raw lanes. Row #28 flipped the checker to STRICT BY DEFAULT (typed-to-typed mismatches are load errors, `var` is the runtime hatch), added `cast<T>/<T&>/<shared_ptr<T>>`, program-closure checking (import order no longer matters for names), `extern` declarations and the `[[warn_mode]]`/`[[strict_mode]]` region attributes; every example, the fuzz differential and Bindstone's `-verifyassets` run strict. Row #29 closed the last two known fuzz parity gaps (callee-before-args and subscript-left-before-lifted-rhs in the vm) and the strict flip's surfaced quirks (user accessors honored, bare C++ properties in hybrids, ONE conversion table for every typed slot, static typing, return-error attribution, a cast-copy warning). Row #30 landed Dev's rulings (return-site catch, every coroutine spelling, yield in any sub-expression on the interpreter, C++ property references bind the member), the diagnostics redesign (docs/diagnostics.md: four families rewritten as a user reads them), 40 new coroutine tests and the checker's accessor typing + precision map. Row #32 landed the class instance plan (defaults + literal initializers seeded in slot order from a chain-versioned memo; the initializer pass skipped for literal-only chains): constructors -7..-17% on the interleaved A/B. Row #33 (workflow, 18 agents) was the CORRECTNESS PASS over every bug the perf rows had surfaced: field initializers copy like declarations, by-value returns copy any lvalue the callee does not own, string builtins resolve slot locals, the catch variable is a block-scoped local, compound member stores evaluate their receiver once on both backends, closures capture slots before globals, map keys are read-only (strings copy-on-write at the ONE in-place write gate - never a per-iteration copy - and the checker rejects every mutation or mutable alias of `kv.first` at load, Dev's ruling), getter-only C++ properties raise on every store. Row #34 made the parallel fork trust the declared type: an object-kind typed array is never demoted, exclusivity comes from the declared class with live-reference counters as the only fallback, parallel_transform reads members of its element and admits value-class elements, the barrier pre-warm is a class-aware walk; the deep three-level shape went from 45 ms parallel vs 45 serial to 13 vs 43, and two pre-existing races in parallel bodies were fixed on the way. Row #35 took the fork's floor apart on a small-N benchmark (a 2048-element region had been SLOWER than serial): the typed-element view is rebuilt only when the slot changes, the element reference is ONE cursor per chunk instead of one holder per element (deep shape 13 -> 8 ms, Vec3 7 -> 3), the thread count adapts to the measured per-element cost with the pool pre-allocated once at min(hardware_concurrency - 1, 6), and a top-level continue means the next element on both backends. Row #36 made each worker run its whole chunk in-script: the body block is grafted into a counter loop whose advance is one opcode, so a region costs one script entry per thread instead of one per element (a 2048-element region 300 -> 160 us against 495 serial; the Vec3 shape 3 -> 1 ms). Row #37 took structs through parallel_transform: worker-created instances register at the join instead of under a lock, the transform runs the same in-script chunk loop with an emit opcode, and a read-only by-value parameter binds the element in place (a 40k Vec3 transform 25 -> 2-3 ms against 33 serial). Row #38 added the zip parallel_for - one loop variable per array, arrays walked in lockstep, the pre-allocated output struct written in place (40k Vec3 in, 40k out: 4-5 ms against 34 serial). Row #39 collapsed parallel_transform onto the zip (one region core, fn's body grafted into the chunk loop, objects flow out): the transform is the inline zip plus its fresh output (1.9-2.0 against 1.5 on 40k Vec3 -> float; 2.9-3.2 before). Row #39 collapsed parallel_transform onto the zip (one region core, fn's body grafted into the chunk loop, objects flow out): the transform is the inline zip plus its fresh output (1.9-2.0 against 1.5 on 40k Vec3 -> float; 2.9-3.2 before). Queued next: method-push prologue parity with the function push
(~1.8 µs per `new` inside a function body — two environments, five allocations, per-field
default enforcement), then operator dispatch in-loop at push-landing sites.

**Rulings received from Dev (2026-09-02) and landed** (rows #21–#23):
1. Map key types ARE enforced on direct stores (`map<string,int> m; m[5] = 1` raises);
   `map<var,V>` takes any key; an `auto` slot infers from a uniform literal, stays dynamic
   for an empty one, and a mixed literal raises (#22).
2. `++`/`--` work on every lvalue, `m["n"]++` included (#21).
3. Class-typed arrays admit subclasses (#23). Before the ruling they were homogeneous by *exact* class
(both backends reject a `Derived` from `array<Base>` on push, declaration and
array-to-array). Dev's target shape `array<CreatureBase> {VoidElemental, LifeElemental}`
   needs subclass admission; the object slot already carries a per-element class tag, so it
   is a boundary-kernel rule change, not a layout change (typed_array_design.md, stage 5).

The language-level decision of the night (Dev, in-session): **the representation follows
the declared type** — `array<int>`/`array<float>`/`array<bool>`/`array<char>`/`array<string>`
are buffers by construction, `array<var>` is the only dynamic form, conversion is an
explicit assignment, nothing demotes silently, no Debug/Release difference, and a uniform
`auto` literal infers its element type. What that implies next (object kind for user types,
typed maps, raw byte lanes, stage-3 parallel reads) is designed in the follow-on section
and in typed_array_design.md stage 5.

**2026-09-08, end of day (rows 67 to 76).** The 8-worker configuration went from 1.52 to about 1.00 to 1.05 ms/tick on GLOOM while serial stayed at 1.37: worker frames read borrowed tables through the decl lanes (row 67), the fixed 16-element region cutoff gave way to the cost model so the wall-ray region fans out at all (69), the pool spins before parking when regions are dense (73), and the partition is block-cyclic so the slowest worker no longer carries the wall band (74). Three correctness rows rode along, all found by instruments rather than by reading: the cross-thread refcount canary named the string-detach hole in one run (70), the runner's new access-violation backtrace named the unsafe-mode interner race in one run (72), and the profile's worker-span line named the partition imbalance (74). Two candidates were measured and rejected with their numbers (75, 76); the 8-worker noise band on this box is about plus or minus 3% per three-round interleaved run, so a claim under 4% needs two runs agreeing in sign. Where the serial floor is now: the hot loops (GLOOM's column paint, JaiDOOM's column draws) are one typed dispatch per statement already, so the next serial step is the design's raw-cell frame and typed calls, a structural campaign, not a row; the region fixed cost (about 40 us of a GLOOM tick is the runtime's share, measured as one worker against none) is a bundle of 1% items. Lua 5.4 remains about 3.5x ahead serially and about 2.6x ahead of the 8-worker configuration on the matched protocol.

**2026-09-10, early (rows 98 to 107).** Closed typed loops run as cell blocks by default (98); element reads land in compiler temps for every scalar consumer (99, 100); reference aliases and typed value parameters feed the parser's typed-store proof (101, 102); the lift's landing-kind read is confined to subscript operands, closing an intermittent access violation that AddressSanitizer named on its first replay (104); hoisted reads keep the interpreter's evaluation order past reference-declared slots (105); slot-resident reference declarations keep a function on the lazy-environment path (106); a reference to a bare name in a method binds the member before a same-named global (107). Astra's audited pair on master ed7c3bd0 against the d6c9959b snapshot (both layouts, 72 of 72 timings conforming; report 5ef0397b, `examples/gloom/ports/RESULTS_FIXED_MASTER_2026-09-10.md`): GLOOM serial 1.3713 to 1.2780 ms/tick normal (-6.81%) and -6.66% padded, 8 workers 0.9612 to 0.8741 (-9.05%) and -11.21%, JaiDOOM 4.6915 to 4.3736 ms/tic (-6.78%) and -8.73%; the delta is cumulative from row 89. Astra's LOAD-cost controls (report ad9d263a, `docs/review-2026-09-09/LOAD_COST_2026-09-10.md`) put an environment read 2.0 to 2.4 ns per site above a local read, which bounds an environment-global fast path to a modest whole-program effect. Serial GLOOM stands at about 3.2x Lua 5.4; Fable's gauge after rows 105 and 106 reads 1.29 ms/tick serial and 4.36 ms/tic JaiDOOM.

## Rules this pass ran under

- 4%+ is the headline bar; consistent, proven 2-3% wins are acceptable when the
  complexity is proportionate (Dev, this session). Anything below that or unproven is
  reverted, and the row says so.
- Zero non-const static / thread_local state. Engines are not internally thread-safe,
  but distinct engines on distinct threads must never touch shared mutable state.
- checked_result stays; starve it (fewer dispatches, cheaper success tests), never
  replace it with throws. Engine code throws only at host boundaries.
- Interned ids everywhere; no strings on hot paths.
- Both backends byte-identical (results, error codes, error text). A concession on
  error text needs a pinned test per backend and a row here. None were needed.
- Interpreter is the oracle and must not lose: every increment re-runs the full suite
  on both backends and the three same-seed hash tripwires on both backends.
- Value semantics stay, at the return too (2026-09-04): a by-value `return e;` moves a
  temporary or the callee's own local/parameter and deep-copies any other lvalue read
  (global, field, element, shared cell, reference result) through the one store kernel;
  `-> T&` returns, `shared_ptr` handles and the top-level `return` are unchanged
  (`detail/return_conv.hpp`, docs/script_extensible.md 6.5).
- Compound stores evaluate their target once (2026-09-04, C++ `a.b op= c`): the receiver
  of a member compound / `++` / `--` (`pk().v += 5`, `arr[side()].v++`, `a.b.c += 1`,
  operator-overloaded `h.v += V(4)`) runs exactly once on both backends - the interpreter
  holds the receiver across the read, the rhs and the store-back (`member_read_on`), the vm
  `op_dup`s it under the read (`op_member_compound` stack `[object, current, rhs]`); a
  yield inside the receiver suspends once and the store lands on the resume. Order stays
  target read -> rhs -> arithmetic -> store. The interpreter's general subscript path lost
  its synthetic `=` re-dispatch: a compound whose target read yields no lvalue raises the
  vm's `Cannot assign to rvalue expression` after the arithmetic - two interpreter-only
  texts aligned to the vm and pinned (`s[i] op= v` on a string, was the read-only-string
  text; `m[k] += "s"` on a missing key, was a silent `"nulls"` insert). Row 30's
  "`pk().v += 5` evaluates the receiver call twice" is resolved.
- **Representation follows the declared type** (Dev, this session, after the typed-storage
  finding): `array<int>`/`array<float>` are raw buffers, period; `array<var>` is the only
  `script_value`-vector form; converting between them is an explicit language step
  (`array<var> x = ints;`); nothing demotes silently (a demote is a full copy the type
  never asked for); no Debug/Release behavior difference; `bound_array` keeps its
  in-place C++ mutation contract; maps follow the same rule (next campaign).

## Measurement protocol

- Wall: GLOOM `jai_gloom.exe --smoke --ticks 600 --seed 666` (serial default, both
  backends printed, min-of-3), JaiDOOM `p10_timedemo.jai` 120x78 (vm, warm jaibite,
  min-of-3), Foundry `Performance Benchmarks` (Release BENCHMARKS, integer µs, min-of-5
  per backend; sub-µs rows are ties by construction).
- Opcode ranking: `out/build/x64-Profile` (`/DJAISCRIPT_VM_PROFILE`), GLOOM 200 ticks vm.
  Profile cycles rank; only Release walls decide.
- Correctness tripwires (must be byte-identical on BOTH backends, every increment):
  GLOOM 600t frame `dfd969ed5a336dca` / STATE `1503537018`; crawler seed 99 1500 turns
  STATE `3849293868`; demoreel 1700 frames `499577b411b67162`; timedemo STATE
  `1746772898`. Bindstone arena pilots run at the end of the pass (needs the client relinked).
- Debug suites (interp + vm) after every change; Release suites before every commit.

## Findings that named the increments

- **STORE @ 282 cyc (5.2% of GLOOM) was ONE store.** A profile-only taxonomy added to
  `exec_store` (cycles by landing path + by symbol name, permanent in the Profile
  dump) attributed 101 of STORE's ~108 Mcyc to 192 stores of `pix` at 527k cycles
  each: `render.jai:331 pix = G.bg` through a `var&` param, one per frame, deep-copying
  an UNTYPED 4000-int array element by element (~130 cyc per scalar element through
  an out-of-line `clone()` call). Slot stores are 38 cyc; env-cell stores are rare
  (31 per 200 ticks). The general fix is in the shared clone kernel (#3).
- **Typed array storage is OFF everywhere except the GLOOM host.** `engine::typed_array_storage_`
  defaults to false (typed_array_design.md: "scaffold flag, default OFF — DELETE when the
  default flips after stage 3 bake"); only `gloom_adapter_jai.cpp` sets it. So in the runner
  (the whole DOOM tape), the test suite and Bindstone, every `array<int>`/`array<float>` is
  32-byte heterogeneous storage: the new copy rows show a typed 1000-int copy at 120 µs vs
  89 µs untyped (a per-element rebuild walk instead of one 8 KB memcpy), and the Release
  micro `typedcopy2.jai` shows 6 ns per element flat across sizes for typed→typed copies.
  (History: #5 measured it behind an opt-in flag; Dev then ruled it the representation and
  #6 removed the flag altogether.)
- The Profile config does not reproduce Release's operand-ladder cost (#1 read as
  neutral in the profile but −1.5% in Release): rank with the profile, decide with
  Release walls and Foundry rows, as the protocol says.

## Follow-on campaign: typed representation for every element type (Dev's direction)

Ruled tonight, not yet built — in the order I would take them:

1. **`array<auto>` inference from a uniform literal.** LANDED as #7 (both `auto` and
   `array<auto>`; mixed literals stay rejected by the validator; empty/nested/object stay
   dynamic).
2. **More primitive kinds**: LANDED as #8 (bool/char byte buffers) and #9 (string handle
   buffer). Byte/string kinds box through get/set in the INDEX family; raw INDEX lanes for
   bool/char (and a `raw_bool` decl sink) are the remaining per-op win there.
3. **Object kind for user types** (`array<CreatureBase>`): node pins the class, stores
   handles (8 bytes), homogeneous by declared class. Boundary: `std::vector<std::shared_ptr<Base>>`
   / `std::vector<Base*>` bind with ONE class check per array (hybrids hand over their
   C++ object pointer), no per-element probing. Needs the hybrid layer's upcast rule and
   the reference holder's element mode for object buffers.
4. **Maps follow the same rule**: `map<string,int>` etc. get typed key/value storage;
   `map<var,var>` is the only dynamic form. Ordering contract (`script_map` stays ordered)
   holds; the win is key compare + value footprint.
5. **parallel_transform stage 3**: workers read typed buffers raw (today: a boxed snapshot
   of a typed input, no demote).
6. ~~Zero-copy `std::vector<script_int>&` parameters~~ — not applicable (#9b): the binder
   rejects container references by design; `bound_array<T>&` is the buffer-binding path.

| 14 | A fresh Debug band (seeds 30000..33999) **stalled** twice at seed 33294: the isolated child ran forever and the driver waited on it. The program's `for` loop is infinite by construction (the generator resets the counter each pass), and with a 250 ms budget the vm stops in 0.36 s while the **interpreter never raises the budget**. Bisected to the fast counting-loop path: when the counter's storage kind changes mid-loop (here a one-time `bump(i)` binding it by reference boxes it into a cell), the path falls through into a continuation `while (true)` that had no back-edge limit check — every iteration after that ran unbudgeted (the deadline was armed exactly once, probe-verified; no re-arm). The same fall-through is reachable by an `[&]` capture of the counter. Fix: the continuation loop runs the same `execution_limit_exhausted()` check as every other loop path. Driver: a child-side wall-clock watchdog (max(10 s, 40× budget)) exits with a HANG code the parent counts and reports, so a hang can never stall a band again | the budget is the host's anti-hang guarantee (Bindstone runs scripts per frame under it); a loop shape that escapes it is a correctness bug, not a perf item | three probe programs and the seed now raise the budget error on the interpreter; 2 pins in the Execution Budget suite (counter boxed by a reference call / by an `[&]` capture, budget 50 ms); Debug 2228×2 green; the isolated driver now reports the seed as a budget-skip in under three seconds; the band re-run to completion on the fixed build (seeds 30000..33999, 4000 programs: 0 divergences, 0 crashes, 0 hangs). Not measured: a check on a cold fall-through path; Release suites + hash tripwires re-run | FIX (interpreter budget hole) + harness watchdog | `6d043f9c` |

| 18 | **O(1) instance unregister.** `~class_instance` called `class_definition::unregister_instance`, which scanned the class's whole `instances_` weak list, locking every entry, to find and erase itself — O(live instances) per destruction, so churn is quadratic in the live population (200 live objects → 40k weak locks per 200-object copy). Each instance now remembers its index in the list; registration writes it, the destructor swap-removes in O(1) and re-points the moved entry's owner, the hot-reload compaction reindexes. Never-registered instances (the deserialization construct-then-classify path) carry the sentinel and skip. | 27 | **Parse-time type proofs (Dev: "enforce what we can at parse/AST time"; workflow-built, 14 agents, ~3.5 h).** The existing opt-in static checker (`detail/type_checker.hpp`, docs/static_checking.md) now runs its walk in EVERY check mode (one walk per unique source per surface epoch, cached beside the parse) and stamps PROOFS onto AST nodes: `same_type` on assignments, declarations, call arguments (single same-script overload, exact arity, no defaults/ref params/coroutines) and returns; field proofs `{proven_class, proven_slot, proven_epoch, proven_field_type, write_ok}` on `member_expr` and bare-name field reads/writes inside methods (receiver class certain, script-only chain, all-public, declared instance field with synthesized accessors); `binary_expr::static_operand_class` as annotation only. Certainty is the complement of the checker's lenient rows (`var`, `auto` without initializer, references, handles, containers, engine globals, includes, callable variables, mixed ternaries are all UNCERTAIN), so the false-positive contract doubles as the proof-soundness contract. Both backends consume proofs behind TOTAL runtime guards (`detail/type_proofs.hpp`: `same_type_payload`, `proven_field_instance`/`proven_field_cell`): a same_type proof skips `enforce_type_compatibility` / `try_convert_for_parameter` / `convert_return_value` / `enforce_field_write`'s conversion; a field proof reaches the cell through class pointer + `field_shape_epoch` guard; any miss runs the old code byte for byte. `engine::type_proofs(bool)` (default on; off strips cached stamps at the next execute), `engine::last_proof_stats()`, `class_definition::field_shape_epoch()` (moves on field-set / declared-type / parent / access / accessor changes and non-identical reloads, NOT on identical reloads), `engine::note_class_shape_change()`. Hot reload: a retyped field misses its proof and converts/resets through migration as today - no path where a proof errors and the unproven path would not. The vm reads decl/call/member/identifier proofs off the node at execution (re-stamps reach compiled chunks); store/return proofs ride instruction flags frozen at compile | The night's own profiles: every inline cache built in rows #24/#26 compensates for facts the compiler has (field slot, declared type, callee), and the same-type conversion ladders still ran on every typed store/bind/return. Why neutral: the rows #24-#26 caches had already removed the hash work the proofs bypass, and v1 excludes the two shapes where a proof would pay today - `shared_ptr<T>` parameters/handles (lenient rows -> uncertain) and containers (container enforcement is not the identity). | 28 | **Strict static checking by default (Dev: "try strict, see what breaks"; three workflows + one agent, ~9 h).** `engine::static_checking` now defaults to `check_mode::strict`: a typed-to-typed mismatch (`int x = "s"`, a string returned from `-> int`, an array into an `int` parameter, an unrelated class into a typed slot) is a LOAD error (`jai::static_check_error`, compiler-style listing) and only a `var`-sourced value can still fail at run time, where `try`/`catch` sees it (Dev's split). Flipping the default surfaced 56 suite failures: 34 checker false positives fixed (`math::` intrinsics, `[this]` captures, handle builtins `use_count`/`unique`/`same_as`/`weak_from_this`, class redefinition across executes, namespaces, `extend()`, null-initialized `auto`, `operator=` overloads, cpp-class member lookup through `derived_defines_member`), 17 tests rewritten to the new contract (both modes tested), the five mode tests re-pointed at the strict default. New spelling for the one legitimate strict failure: `cast<T>(e)` (what `T tmp = e;` would hold), `cast<T&>(e)` (a reference - `cast<Derived&>(h.p).t = 5` stores through), `cast<shared_ptr<T>>(e)` (shares the instance); class mismatch raises the catchable `Cannot cast 'X' to 'T'`; char converts only through the cast (`cast<int>('a') == 97`, `cast<char>(97)` range-checked 0..255, `cast<string>(c)`, `cast<char>("a")`), shared kernel `detail/cast_kernel.hpp`, `cast` is a keyword. **Programs:** a unit whose top-level imports carry literal paths is a program root; before anything runs the engine gathers the closure (each unit once, cycles fine, `importing_` guard), unions every unit's top-level names (functions, variables, classes, enums, namespaces with `::` prefixes) into the engine's program-name memory and checks root + closure against it, so a leaf may use a global another file declares whatever the import order (GLOOM's `maps.jai`/`G` case - GLOOM failed to LOAD under strict before this). Undeclared identifiers are errors except in units reached through a dynamic-path import. The closure carries class MODELS too (one modeler, `model_class()`, for same-unit and program-table classes): a subclass whose parent lives in another unit resolves inherited fields/methods bare, through `this`/`self` and typed receivers, down to a registered C++ base (`Whisper -> CreatureKind -> ServerCreature`: the 12 creature scripts that failed the first strict `-verifyassets` with the parent's own fields reported undeclared); program-table types are `tentative` - typed for diagnostics, never a proof source - until the declaring class registers, and an ancestor found nowhere makes bare names in the subclass lenient. **Escape hatches:** `extern function name;` / `extern <type> name;` / `extern name;` declares a name defined outside the program (checker: declared, typed -> checked and proven; both backends: a no-op) - the spelling for host-registered names (`spawnOnNetwork`, whose C++ stub in `-verifyassets` is REMOVED) and for a shared leaf calling an optional sibling module (JaiDOOM's `things/game/hud/spec.jai` declare the 16 `np_*` names `netplay.jai` defines; the timedemo tape hash holds). `[[warn_mode]]` / `[[strict_mode]]` (Dev's final shape, replacing a unit-only `[[skip_strict]]`): first declaration of a file = the unit; before a statement = that one statement's subtree, then the mode reverts, and a nested lambda/function body keeps the ENCLOSING mode; first statement of a block = the whole block (nested bodies included); innermost wins; engine `off` ignores them; the runtime never changes (a warn region's type mistakes still raise). Flag on the `statement` base, one byte per node in `.jaibite` (8 -> 11 across the increment: v9 cast node, v10 extern, v11 mode flag). Both seeds now agree. Rejection texts spell the payload's dynamic type (`got string`, never `got any`/`got this`). (6) **Static field typing** (`detail/static_store.hpp`): every supported spelling (`C::s`, bare `s` in instance/static methods, ctors, initializers) enforces the declared type through the same table with `Type mismatch in assignment`; typed static initializers convert; `C::s op= v` and `++C::s` are lvalues on both backends; a nested-block bare static store in a static method bypassed enforcement in the interpreter (fixed); `C.s` is NOT a spelling and now raises the same string on both backends. with `live_elem_refs == 0` the whole normalization is skipped and no element is touched (`verdict: ... -> declared-type proof, no element walk`; normalize 117 Mcyc -> 0.2 Mcyc); `reference_holder::elem_root` pins the outermost array a ref-lvalue chain descended through (`arr[i].kids[j].v`) so nested refs count on the array; workers read the source live and the by-value parameter binding makes the private copy, the per-element `parallel_detached_copy` + slot input vector are gone; a soundness hole closed on the way (a shared_ptr-tagged instance with a live alias was admitted by the reference-free fast path: two chunks could hold one instance); The x64-Profile split (6 workers) showed setup 271k cyc, submit 108k, main-chunk 617k (1800 cyc per element vs ~700 for the serial body), and half the opcode's self-time OUTSIDE the profiled window: the join guard destroying 2048 pre-minted element references. Landed, in order: (1) **typed-element view** - a by-ref loop variable over an object-kind (or string-kind) typed array rebuilt its boxed view on EVERY touch (a handle copy + a release; eight field touches per iteration = eight rebuilds); `script_array::view_current` + a fast head in `materialize_typed_element` keep the scratch while the slot still holds the same handle and tag (touch.jai, 6.4M touches, vm: typed 335 -> 315 ms vs boxed 285; the residual ~4 ns per touch is the compare itself); (2) **cursor references** - the barrier mints ONE element reference per chunk instead of one per element and the worker re-points its `container_index` before each call (`script_value::element_cursor_holder`: the holder this handle alone owns, ELEMENT mode; a retained holder is an internal error, impossible under admission: no lambdas, no `new`, stores deref, captured state read-only, result discarded); the serial by-ref range-for does the same on both backends for frame-slot loops (the previous iteration's holder moves to the next index when `unique()`, else a fresh mint - a `[&]` alias keeps its element; top-level loops keep the per-iteration mint so destructor timing stays byte-parallel between backends): N=2048 842 -> 280 us, callcost inline 682 -> 210, **shapes deep 13 -> 8 ms parallel (serial 44), Vec3 7 -> 3 (serial 8)**, touch.jai typed 332 -> 298 / boxed 289 -> 275 (vm), interpreter 865 -> 825 / 817 -> 787; (3) **cost-adaptive thread count** - a fixed 32-elements-per-thread cap fixed N=32 (37 -> 17 us) but broke GLOOM's 40 heavy glyph rows (vm W=4 2.2 -> 3.2 ms/tick: one worker), and a first attempt at measuring the per-worker overhead from each region's own fork wall fed back on itself (straggler time shrinks with fewer workers) and got trapped by the pool's cold first wake (86 us per worker measured once, then a one-worker region never produces a new sample; N=256 sat at 120 us). Final shape (Dev: adaptive is fine, first pass = simple even split as the baseline, machine agnostic): the fixed per-worker cost is measured ONCE when the pool is built (`calibrate_pool_overhead`: three no-op round trips, the minimum; 4.1 us here) and each statement's region pool keeps a half-life-one smoothed ns per element of main's own chunk (`note_region_cost`); a statement's first region uses every thread, later regions wake only workers whose chunk is worth 3x the wake cost (`k_parallel_chunk_overhead_ratio`); the pool lookup (`acquire_region_pool`) moved ahead of the thread-count decision at both forks. GLOOM back to 2.16 at W=4 / 2.12 at W=6 vs 2.29 serial, N=256 52-57 vs 61, N=2048 275-325 vs 500, N=32 17.5 (one context); (4) **pool sizing and default** - the pool was built with `max(needed, hardware_concurrency - 1)` = 23 threads on this box for 3 ever used; now the engine's worker count minus main, pre-allocated once and reused (Dev: a consistent pre-allocated set), default `min(hardware_concurrency - 1, 6)` (Dev); (5) **body control flow** - a top-level `continue` in a parallel body raised `'continue' statement not in loop` on the vm and was silently ignored on the interpreter (fell through to the next statement); it now means the next element on both backends (rewritten to a bare `return` once at body-function creation, `parallel_body_control_rewrite`), a loop-level `break` is rejected at admission with `parallel_for: 'break' is not allowed in a parallel body (iterations are independent; use 'continue' for the next element) at line:col`, nested loops and switches keep their own. | 36 | **parallel_for: the worker runs its chunk in-script (Dev: "It would be better to batch the script itself and iterate without exiting/entering the script ... running the for loop fully in-script without enter/exit boundaries 4 times instead of n times on each thread").** Before: each element was one host-to-script call on the worker (`execute_callable` -> `call_script_function`: a callee environment allocated and the run loop re-entered per element, ~1800 cyc against ~700 for the same body in a serial range-for), which is why regions under a few hundred light elements lost to serial. First cut (measured, not kept as-is): a parsed wrapper `for (k...) { __jai_parallel_body(__c); __jai_parallel_advance(); }` still paid an in-loop call (~600 cyc: push + probe + halt) plus a NATIVE advance call (~500 cyc) per element - the worker profile put CALL_FROM_SCRATCH at 45% of its time - so main-chunk went UP to 2700 cyc per element. Landed: (1) the body is GRAFTED into the chunk loop - `build_chunk_wrapper` parses a per-statement skeleton (`k_parallel_chunk_inline_ref` / `_val`), makes the loop parameter itself parameter 0 (the body's own slot 0, same name; `__n`/`__k` renumbered past the body's frame by name, `local_count + 2`) and replaces the placeholder with the body block, so a worker enters script once per region and runs zero calls per element; (2) two vm opcodes replace the intrinsics - `op_parallel_advance` (pops the counter, re-points the worker's cursor, pushes counter + 1; `__k = __jai_parallel_advance(__k)` sits in the for-update so a `continue` reaches it) and `op_parallel_element` (pushes the cursor for the by-value form's `__c = __jai_parallel_element()`), compiled from the reserved names in `compile_call`; the interpreter keeps them as per-worker natives (`provision_worker` defines both plus `__jai_parallel_body`); `vm_backend::set_parallel_cursor` hands the worker its `std::optional<script_value>` cursor for the region; (3) control flow by mode - inline mode rewrites a top-level `return` to `continue` (top-level `continue` already lands on the advance), a body with a `return` inside a NESTED loop takes call mode (`k_parallel_chunk_call`: `__jai_parallel_body(__c)` per element, the previous shape) so its meaning holds (`scan_parallel_body_control` / `rewrite_parallel_body_control`, `parallel_for_stmt::chunk_mode` 1/2, `chunk_decl_cache`); the loop-level `break` rejection stays; (4) by-value bodies also run through the cursor (they copy through it exactly as they copied the element; `raw_input`/`typed_input` are transform-only now). Floor benchmark (vm, us per region, parallel vs serial, min of 3): N=32 17.5 -> 14 vs 7.8, N=256 52-57 -> 38-52 vs 60, **N=2048 262-325 -> 155-190 vs 495**; callcost inline body 195 -> 115 (serial 427), with a call 250 -> 210 (serial 567); shapes Vec3 **3 -> 1 ms** (serial 8-11), deep 8-9 (unchanged: the nested member chain is the body), flat 3 (unchanged). Worker per-element cost is now ~1.3x the serial body for an inline body (the cursor deref + counter loop); the remaining fixed cost per region is the wake (~4 us per worker) and a region's provisioning. Behavior note: a typed by-value loop variable now converts through assignment (`c = element`) rather than parameter binding, the SAME conversion table; a mismatch inside a `var` array raises the assignment text instead of the parameter text (strict rejects typed sources at load). NEXT: the by-value form pays one extra clone per chunk (parameter bind + first assignment); primitive kinds still demote at the fork (typed stage 3); transform over objects (registry churn per copy); transform method admission; GLOOM's `var` arrays and DOOM's blit rule are script-side follow-ups. | Dev: "Let's do that next lever. | 37 | **Structs through parallel_transform: registry churn off the workers, the transform on the in-script chunk loop, read-only parameters bind in place (Dev: "I want to optimize for iterating structs that we can output to in parallel"; "with the parallel_transform, we should be able to use a segmented iterate in place similar to how Bindstone's emitter/particles do"; "Is there a world in which parallel_for IS parallel_transform?").** Measured first (Release vm, 40k elements, ms per region parallel vs serial): a transform over `Vec3` (`v.x * v.y`) ran 25 vs 33 and over the deep node shape 241 vs 252 - barely parallel - because every worker copy of an element (the by-value parameter) and every instance a worker constructed took the class registry's region mutex to register and again to unregister, and because each element was still one host-to-script call. Landed: (1) **deferred registration** - `class_definition::register_instance` on a pool worker parks the weak handle in the worker's pending list (`parallel_region_table::entry::pending_registrations`, `engine::parallel_pending_registrations` by thread id) instead of locking; a never-registered or pending instance dies without touching the registry (`unregister_instance` returns before the lock on `registry_slot_ == SIZE_MAX`); the join registers the survivors on the main thread (`register_instance_after_region`) so hot reload still migrates everything that lived (pinned: `worker_copied_instances_register_at_the_join` reloads a class after a parallel copy and reads the new field on the copy); main's own chunk keeps the lock against a worker's rare death of a registered instance. Alone: Vec3 25 -> 6, deep 241 -> 128. (2) **the transform runs the chunk loop in-script** - `k_parallel_chunk_transform` (`for (__k...) { __jai_parallel_emit(__jai_parallel_fn(__c)); }`, parsed once per engine, provisioned per worker with fn under `__jai_parallel_fn`), the chunk's cursor minted at the barrier on the source node (a primitive-typed input is boxed once into a private node the cursor can address, the same boxed copies as before; object and untyped inputs are addressed in place), and a third opcode `op_parallel_emit` that hands fn's result to the fork's sink (`transform_emit_sink`: `result_value_semantic` + the snapshot-alias detach, then the disjoint write into the pre-sized output; a per-worker native on the interpreter); the failing element is the cursor's index. (3) **read-only parameters bind in place** - the admission walk records `element_mutated` (a member store rooted at the element, a non-read-only method on an element-rooted chain) beside the existing `element_reach_all` (the element used whole: stored, passed, compared, returned, aliased); when neither is set, the worker's private copy of fn gets its by-value element parameter flipped to by-reference (`provision_worker`), so a read-only fn reads the element in place with no copy and no observable difference - a fn that writes through its parameter, hands it to a `T&` helper, mutates a field container, or uses it whole keeps the copy (pinned per shape: `read_only_fn_binds_in_place_mutating_shapes_still_copy`, source unchanged in every case). The same rule gives a by-value `parallel_for` body that only reads the by-ref skeleton (in place, no per-element copy). After: **Vec3 transform 25 -> 2-3 ms vs 33 serial, deep 241 -> 86 vs 252**; parallel_for shapes deep 8 -> 7, N=256 floor 45 -> 36 vs 61. Suites 2572/2572 both backends; GLOOM parity at auto/4/6. Two pre-existing LIMITS the new tests hit, filed as the next pass ("structs out"): fn may not return an object (`fn returned a non-value-semantic result (object)`) and constructors are not admitted in a parallel body (`call target 'K' is not a plain function (v0)`), so a worker cannot build struct results yet even though deferred registration now makes it safe. | 38 | **The zip `parallel_for` (Dev: "What if we had a pre-allocated array of structs that we assign to? | 41 | **String tables join the borrow tier (Dev: "is there a way to avoid deep copying, instead sharing the same root array as storage?").** A captured container whose elements are primitives OR strings, touched only by subscript reads, is now a borrow: workers read the live root storage through the tagged raw pointer and a string read mints the BYTES into a fresh worker-owned string (`script_value(script_string(*handle), eng)`), never copying the live handle, so the non-atomic count is never touched from a worker. Landed in the one shared kernel `parallel_borrow_subscript_read` (typed str buffer, hetero string elements, map string values), in `parallel_content_all_primitive` (typed kinds other than obj, hetero elements incl. `TYPEID_STRING`, map keys/values) and in `statically_all_primitive` (declared `string` element/key/value types), so a `var` string table and a typed `array<string>` take the same tier. Borrow materialization of a typed array (the result path's silent deep clone) goes through the same `detached_typed_array` helper as the snapshot detach, so it no longer walks the empty hetero vector of a typed node. Nested containers and object elements stay snapshots. | Row 40 made typed string captures correct by deep-copying every entry per worker per region: GLOOM's palettes (hundreds of escape strings) were copied 4-8 times per row region, and the workers ran SLOWER than serial (vm 2.45 at 4 workers vs 2.27 serial). The old rationale for the snapshot tier ("dense re-reads would pay a per-read deep clone under a borrow") does not hold for strings: a palette read is a ~16-byte copy, the table copy is hundreds of them, and the `var` table paid an O(n) content proof per region on top to reuse its cached copy. Sharing the root storage read-only is exactly what the numeric borrow already did; the only hazard was the handle count, and copying bytes instead of the handle removes it. | GLOOM 300 ticks seed 666, interleaved A/B row-40 binary vs this, same typed scripts, min of 3 ms/tick: interpreter W=0 9.00 -> 9.09 (+1.0%), W=4 7.56 -> 6.13 (-19.0%), W=8 7.89 -> 5.70 (-27.8%); vm W=0 2.27 -> 2.27 (-0.1%), W=4 2.45 -> 2.01 (-17.9%), W=8 2.76 -> 1.92 (-30.6%). | 42 | **Read-only by-value parameter elision (Dev: "Great, let's do that!" after the row-41 recap named it the next structure question).** A by-value class/array/map parameter that the callee provably only reads binds the argument by handle instead of a deep clone, on both backends and in serial code as much as in parallel bodies. The proof is the parallel admission walk in a new `proof_mode` (`prove_readonly_parameters`, `parallel_transform.cpp`): the callee and every script function it calls would be admitted in a parallel body (writes nothing but its own locals; no host call outside the pure whitelist, no lambda/new/yield), and with the parameter as element root nothing identity-observing touches it - `element_mutated` as before plus `root_escaped` on a bare use (comparison, argument, return, range source), an alias, a compound assignment or ++/-- on it, a user method or accessor on it, or a member/subscript chain whose static terminal is not a primitive or string (objects and arrays compare by identity). Read-only builtins on a receiver that is statically a container or string are reads; `p = x` rebinding is not an escape. Cached per `script_defined_function` (`alias_candidate`, `alias_proof_epoch/mask`) against the new `engine::definition_epoch()` (moves on every `execute_parsed`, function/class/global definition and host registration); parallel workers never prove - the barrier proves the live function and restamps each worker copy per region entry (`restamp_alias_proofs`). Consumed at both backends' exact-class fast branch and post-conversion store (`script_value(derefed)` vs `.clone()`), gated on the argument by `parameter_alias_allows` (script class instances that are not nocopy, arrays, maps; host objects clone). Plain functions only in v1 (methods/coroutines bind through other paths). The `.jaibite` sibling stored absolute filename stamps, so a moved folder would have answered with the old location (and traced the old paths): the writer now marks the unit's own file and the reader rebinds the marker to the path the bite is loaded under (format 14; the standalone `jaibite::save` path is unchanged). | 74 | **Block-cyclic partition: the elements are dealt to the workers, not sliced.** The span profile at 8 workers after row 73 (VMPROF, P-core pinned) put the row transform's slowest worker at 436k to 446k cycles against 252k to 254k for the fastest and 238k to 245k for main: contiguous chunks hand main the ceiling rows and the middle workers the wall-heavy band, and the region ends when the heaviest chunk does. Without a weight hint the partition is now block-cyclic: blocks of max(1, n / 4W) consecutive elements dealt round-robin over the W threads (n = 34, W = 8: blocks of one; n = 64, W = 4: blocks of four), so a cost that varies along the range spreads over every thread; the cursor steps by the block rule on both backends, the zip's cursors together, a chunk's length is its count, and the weight hint keeps its contiguous cumulative split. Small n means expensive elements (the cost cap keeps cheap small ranges on one thread) and large n means blocks of hundreds, so no two threads write neighbouring scalars. | 75 | **Pool hand-off without the convoy: padded spin lines, a lock per pinned queue, a lock-free completion.** After row 73 the submit phase still cost 19k to 26k cycles per region and wake latency still averaged 13k to 17k with seven spinners: the flags they polled shared a cache line with the mutex and the outstanding count, and every worker that saw its flag then queued behind one pool mutex to pop, seven deep. The polled atomics now sit on their own lines (the per-worker flag on the worker's own), each worker's pinned queue has its own lock taken inside the pool mutex when both are needed (flag == queue non-empty holds under that lock, so a stale flag cannot spin a worker), and a completion decrements the count and pings the joiner only if the joiner is parked, with a seq_cst pair standing in for the lock (either the worker sees the parked flag or the joiner's predicate sees the decrement). Padding alone measured -1.8% at 8 workers (one interleaved run). At this region size the hand-off is inside the noise; the critical path is the slowest worker's chunk plus the region's setup, not the lock. | 83 | **The instance registry's swap-remove race (fix; the 512 -> 511 flake Astra reproduced under load).** `Parallel Registry Faults` lost exactly one live instance about once per 150 runs under CPU load, on either backend, in three sibling shapes of `check_pending_recovery`. The registry is a vector of weak pointers with each instance remembering its index; `~class_instance` swap-removes by that index under the region lock. But an instance's weak entry expires the moment its last owner lets go, BEFORE its destructor takes the lock, and while a region runs another instance dying under the lock swap-removes across it: the expired entry moves (its owner still remembers the old index), or the tail pops and a fresh registration from main's own chunk refills that index with a live instance. The late destructor then removes whatever sits at its remembered index, a live instance's entry, and the count is one short. `unregister_instance` now removes an entry only when it is this instance's own (control-block identity through `weak_from_this`, which survives expiry) and pops expired tails before the swap so whatever moves into the hole is live and re-points; a mismatch leaves an expired weak that every reader skips. Stress loop (ten busy-loop burners, the suite on both backends): before the fix 3 losses in 900 runs (2 vm, 1 interpreter), after it 0 in 1200. | 85 | **Unsafe parallel mode keeps every runtime-owned protection (fix; Astra's unsafe matrix).** Six one-class shapes under `allow_unsafe_parallel(true)` at 8 workers: a sibling call spelled `this.inner()`, a field's method `leaf.read()` and an accessor `this.x` asserted a getter-id miss on the frozen interner on both backends, and a bare sibling call `inner()` died on a dead `strong_ptr` control block. The cause was one skip: unsafe mode returned from the element proof before collecting the element classes, which starved the barrier's element-method walk, so no name was warmed and no method pinned, and the worker resolved methods live through shared counts. Now the admission walk runs in unsafe mode with its script-semantic verdicts lenient (`fail` keeps walking) and its engine-seam rules kept as walls (`wall`: bare sibling calls, call targets the walk cannot resolve, `yield`, includes and declarations in a body, `math::random`); a method the barrier cannot pin is skipped rather than fatal; the element proof collects classes and detaches shared strings in unsafe mode with a lenient verdict; and the worker walls (an unpinned method call, operator dispatch on instances, bound-method and constructor thunks, accessor reads and stores) apply in every mode, their texts no longer promising an override. What unsafe buys is the skipped proof, never a way past a shared count. The last shape exposed a second, older hole that safe mode shares: a pinned method ran with the class's definition environment, so a captured string table read inside a method body resolved to the LIVE global and copied the shared literal's handle across threads (2 of 12 safe interpreter runs died at teardown on the dead block); the pin's environment is now the worker's own sandbox, where the provisioned copies live. Regression: the six shapes on both backends in unsafe mode, plus the runner's new `--unsafe-parallel` switch and the frozen-interner tripwire naming the missing symbol in Debug. | 99 | **Element reads inside typed expressions land in compiler temps (Astra's compiler-coverage finding, `docs/review-2026-09-09/TECHNIQUE_CONTROLS.md`).** The expression form `acc = acc + data[idx]` ran a general fused binary with a boxed element copy while the two-statement form took `T_LOAD`, and the same gap kept such loops out of the cell path. | 100 | **Element reads land in compiler temps for every scalar consumer (the general form of row 99's mechanism).** A subscript expression the fused index op can read in one instruction (an identifier container with an identifier or literal index, a member-base container, or an identifier container with a computed index chain) compiles into a compiler temp through `op_index_fused_decl` with `decl_temp` (no declaration node; its general form is the read plus a slot store; the readers of the node index skip a temp), and the consumer takes the temp as a direct slot operand: a binary's operand (the flat path and the register lift), the value of an element store (`a[i] = b[j]`), the index of an outer read (`a[b[i]]`). The typed lowering gives the read the `T_LOAD` row: the proven kind when the checker proved the binary, the runtime kind otherwise, landing raw and in place with the canonical stamp of the element's kind, so an unknown container (a `var&` parameter over an `array<int>`) stops boxing its reads; a non-scalar element lands boxed in the temp. The read happens before its partner only when the partner cannot raise first (a slot, a literal, a temp, another such read), at every node of a lifted tree, so the interpreter's error order stands. Row 99's operand-temp materializer is removed (subsumed). Micro (4M iterations): the expression form stays at the typed-local form's speed (57.3 to 63.1 against 56.7 to 61.4 ms per 4M iterations over three rounds). | 105 | **Hoisted element reads keep the interpreter's evaluation order past reference-declared slots (fix; Astra's deferred-getter finding: two confirmed row 100 divergences on master).** Row 100 (and the held row 103) lands an element read in a temp before the op that consumes it, and `quiet_partner` admitted any frame-slot identifier as an operand that cannot raise ahead of the read; a slot naming a reference declaration (`var&`, `T&`, a by-reference parameter) can hold a property reference whose dereference runs a getter, which the interpreter runs first (`p[Q[i]]` and `n + Q[i]` with `p` / `n` bound to a throwing C++ property reported the inner bounds error on the VM with proofs on). Every emission site now materializes such an earlier-evaluated operand into a temp with `op_operand_temp` before the hoisted read (the flat pre-step's left partner, the nested-index arm's container, the lift's left operand ahead of a temp-only read or a lifting subtree; the held row 103 arm carries the same rule), so the getter runs once in its place and the consumer reads the temp. A reference bound to a value the unit declares (a value slot, a program-level value declaration, an alias already plain; the parser's `identifier_expr::names_known_value`, re-derived on a jaibite load like `names_value_decl`, and the compiler's per-callable `plain_refs`) dereferences a cell and takes no snapshot, so same-unit aliases keep their cell blocks; the store-value hoist is unchanged because an index store evaluates its right-hand side before dereferencing the target on both backends (Astra's 20-run matrix). | 107 | **A reference to a bare name in a method binds the frame's own member before a same-named global (fix; Astra's follow-up on row 105 found it).** The VM's `exec_decl_ref_ident` and `exec_ref_return_bind` consulted the environment before the frame's members, the reverse of the read order both backends share (locals, then members, then globals); in an eager method frame the method environment's kind fallback still found a script field first but never a C++ property of the chain's bound base, and in a lazy method frame (row 106) the environment is the caller's, so `var& p = values;` inside a hybrid method bound the program-level `values` while the interpreter bound the property. In a method frame both sites now resolve a field or static by pointer, then mint a hybrid's bare C++ property, then fall back to the environment; a plain function keeps the environment first and the mint as the last resort. The parser's plain-reference proof (row 105) also stops at a class body (a program-scope hit inside a class body no longer counts as a known value, since a member or a native property of the same name resolves first there); the compiler needs no change. | 108 | **A statement-position `++x` / `x--` on a frame slot lowers to the int compound row (the first item of the queue agreed with Astra).** The general `op_incdec` resolved its slot, dereferenced, switched on the type, pushed the result and had the dispatch loop pop it again (JaiDOOM: 38,870,503 executions per timedemo at 40.8 cycles each, 2.01% of exclusive time; GLOOM serial: 1,349,683 per 600 ticks at 46.7, 1.26%), while the same slot written as `x += 1` already took `op_t_add_ii` with the compound landing. `lower_typed_ops` now rewrites a no-result, non-`::` increment or decrement whose target is a direct frame slot into `op_t_add_ii` / `op_t_sub_ii` (compound landing, the constant 1 minted once per chunk); the slot's tag is the only guard, and a miss (a reference or shared cell in the slot, a C++-bound value, a float, an overflow) runs the original `op_incdec` through the typed-site fallback, which pops the value the general op pushes, so texts, evaluation order and the overflow policy are the general op's. An increment that was the only general instruction in an otherwise typed loop now lets the loop close as a cell; neither GLOOM nor JaiDOOM has such a loop today (draw_billboard's inner loop keeps an interior typed compare-jump guarding its store), so the row is the per-op saving alone. | 110 | **An index expression that is an arithmetic tree computes into a compiler temp and the read takes the typed row (the shape row 109 left general: `draw_flat_col`'s `flat[((math::itrunc(wy) & 63) << 6) + (math::itrunc(wx) & 63)]` and the `palcm[...]` read around it, two general `INDEX` per pixel).** `try_emit_fused_index` fused an identifier container with an identifier or literal index, a left-spine chain with identifier or literal rights, or (row 103) a flat binary with an element-read operand; any other index tree went through the general `INDEX` with the tree pushed by the fused binary. | 111 | **The order snapshot of a reference-slot container ahead of a hoisted read becomes a guard (part 2 of the row 103c design note; the last 48.4M `OPERAND_TEMP` per timedemo).** Row 105's rule kept the interpreter's container-before-index order by dereferencing a reference-slot container into a temp before every hoisted read (`op_operand_temp`, a dispatch, a dereference and a handle copy per read, about 45 to 62 cycles); after rows 103 and 110 every read through a `var&` parameter or a `var&` alias the parser cannot prove a value paid it. The guard (`op_operand_temp` with `c = 1`, `guard_container_temp`) materializes the container only when the slot's reference is not a plain cell (`reference_holder::plain_cell`: the owned cell with no container, field, map key or property behind it, the one mode whose dereference cannot raise); the read's container operand stays the slot, and the three readers (`typed_load_fast`, `typed_decl_read`, `index_fused_read`) take the guard's temp when the slot holds a non-plain reference and the slot itself otherwise, so a getter still runs exactly once and in its place, an element reference of a shrunken array still raises before the index, and a plain alias pays one tag test in the guard and one in the reader. Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 110 binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout only, hashes held): GLOOM serial 1.2653 -> 1.2744 ms/tick (+0.72%, paired +0.34 / +0.64 / +1.68 / +0.46 / +0.91 / +0.47; the instruction census is identical, the same size as the row 107 layout reading), JaiDOOM 3.84 -> 3.77 ms/tic (-1.82%, paired -2.08 / -1.82 / -1.04 / -1.57 / -1.83 / -1.82), workers 8 0.8510 -> 0.8605 ms/tick (+1.11%; paired +1.31 / -0.62 / +1.05 / +0.92). | 112 | **A closed typed loop may branch inside itself (the first bounded shape of Astra's branching-cell brief: the guarded-store diamond).** The cell recognizer closed on any interior branch, so JaiDOOM's texture column loop (`draw_tex_col`, 22.2M iterations per timedemo, all typed after rows 103 to 111 except `if (iv < 0) { iv = iv + th; }` and its container guard) and GLOOM's `draw_billboard` inner loop (1.22M per 600 ticks, a typed compare-jump guarding its store) ran every iteration through the dispatcher. A typed compare-jump or a jump whose target lies forward inside the loop or at its back edge is now admitted and copied as a cell branch (`op_c_jcmp`: the head's comparison, a false result skipping to the copy's target instead of leaving; `op_c_jump`), so one-arm and two-arm diamonds over typed work, a `continue` and a guarded store close; a branch that leaves the loop (a `break`) keeps it open. A container guard inside the loop (row 111) is a no-op in the copy (`op_c_nop`): the entry resolves the container once and refuses a reference that is not a plain cell, so the general form keeps a getter's per-read call. The copy stays one instruction per original, which is what the miss path's original-ip arithmetic relies on; a kind miss at an interior compare flushes and re-runs the original compare-jump like every other cell miss. The nested read `a[b[i]]` (an identifier index) is admitted as a hoisted operand as well, the shape the row 103c note left open, so a store whose value is such a read keeps a temp operand and the loop around it can close. A temp that holds an element the checker could not class (a read through a `var&` container, `decl_expect` any) is a cell too: its kind is pinned by a typed consumer (an index fold, a compare) or adopted at block entry from the slot's current value (the general form's first iteration landed it), the cell load lands the element's own kind, and every consumer checks the kind at run time as it does for any cell, so a mismatch is a miss onto the general form; without this the texture loop's reads (`tp[base + iv]`, `palcm[...]` through `var&`) could not be cells at all. Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 111 binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout only, hashes held): GLOOM serial 1.2651 -> 1.2051 ms/tick (-4.74%, paired -5.49 / -4.84 / -4.56 / -5.60 / -4.24 / -4.31), workers 8 0.8635 -> 0.8024 ms/tick (-7.08%, paired -9.14 / -6.89 / -6.92 / -6.45), JaiDOOM 3.77 -> 3.715 ms/tic (-1.46%, paired -1.59 / -1.33 / -1.59 / -1.59 / -1.05 / -1.57). Census: GLOOM per 600 ticks: cell entries 0 -> 87,594, cell iterations 3,253,718, misses 0, refusals 0; dispatched instructions 62,578,445 -> 52,485,449 (T_LOAD -1.45M, T_MUL_II, T_ADD_II, T_SUB_II, T_DIV_II, T_JGT_II and T_JLT_II each -1.2M, LOOP_BACK -1.22M: draw_billboard's inner loop runs as a block); JaiDOOM: cell entries stay 0 (the texture loop's store index reads the global VW from the environment, which a cell cannot take yet), INDEX_FUSED 36,044,801 -> 35,564,680 with T_LOAD up by the same 480,121 (the nested read a[b[i]] on the typed row), every other count identical. Controls (suite `Cell Loops`, 1 new; Astra's fixtures from `out/astra/queue-20260910/branch-controls.json` in while form): a two-arm diamond over zero, one and six iterations, the texture loop's guarded arm over `var&` containers, a `continue`, a `break` (the loop stays open), an overflow inside the taken arm (the same text and the same visible state on both backends), proofs on and off; the listings show the diamond and the texture shape closing with `C_JCMP`, `C_JUMP` and `C_NOP`, and the `break` loop staying open. | 113 | **An environment scalar read by an index store inside a closed loop is a read-only cell resolved once at block entry (JaiDOOM's `pix[y * VW + x]`, the last thing keeping `draw_tex_col`'s 22.2M texture iterations per timedemo off the cell path).** A cell body has no call and no store that could change a global such as the view width, so the recognizer registers such an operand as an env cell with a synthetic slot above the frame's, the copy's store reads a cloned proto whose environment operands are direct synthetic-slot operands (the original proto stays the general form's), the entry resolves the scalar through the nullable fast head and unboxes it (an int, else the block is refused and the general form runs), and the flush never writes it. | 114 | **Cell arithmetic whose kinds the cells decide at run time, cell moves, cell math and pinned texture tables: JaiDOOM's `draw_flat_col` (the flat floor and ceiling columns, 8.3M iterations per timedemo) runs as a block.** The flat column loop stayed general for four reasons, each now a cell rule. A fused binary the typed heads left general (an environment scalar operand such as `CENTERY` or `VIEW_X`, or the float by int division `dz / dy`) landing in a stamped scalar decl, a temp or a no-result frame-slot store joins a block as `op_c_arith_any`: the general kernel's int rows, float rows and the int to float promotion of the four arithmetic operators, an overflow, a zero divisor or a bitwise float missing onto the general form; the landing judges the kind against the destination cell's (a non-temp cell holds its slot's kind from the entry, so a body-defined cell is unboxed too): the same kind lands, a float declaration widens an int, a temp takes any kind, anything else is a miss, which is how a declared-type conversion (the `float f = 0.0; f = y + 3` control) stays the general form's. An environment cell adopts int or float from its value at entry unless a typed use pins it, through the operand's own cache slot. Typed moves (`T_STORE_CONST`, `T_STORE_MOV`, `T_DECL_CONST`, `T_DECL_MOV`: the band clamps) copy as `op_c_mov`; `math::itrunc` and `math::ifloor` of a cell run the kernel's conversion in the block as `op_c_math` (an int passes through); an intrinsic's binary argument lands in a temp (`math::itrunc(z * 0.001953125)` is two cell ops instead of a pushing binary and a stack-fed intrinsic). Compiler temps are cells without an entry guard or unbox: temps are reused per statement, so at the back edge a temp's slot may hold another statement's value (the first build refused 8.8M entries per timedemo on the container guard's handle sitting in an arithmetic temp's slot); the copy's guard (`op_c_guard`, formerly `op_c_nop`) clears the dirty bit of a dead temp cell aliasing the guard slot so a later miss's flush leaves the handle the general form reads there. A guarded container that is an element reference into a hetero array (`var& cflat = FLATS[cslot]` passed as `var& flat`, the table entry shape row 112's entry refused) is resolved once at entry like a plain cell when no store site of the loop writes that table (a pointer check against the store nodes; a store into the table refuses the block, since the general form re-reads the entry at every guard), and the entry writes the handle into the guard slot. | 115 | **Counted loops run as cell blocks: `for (int i = lit; i cmp end; i += step)`, the `op_cfor_prep` / `op_cfor_back` form every literal-start loop compiles to, closes under the cell rules (JaiDOOM's `pix_clear`, 37.3M iterations per timedemo, and the small counted loops of both programs).** The cell recognizer only knew the compare-headed loop (`op_loop_back` under a typed compare-jump), so the most common loop spelling never became a block whatever its body held. A counted loop's body from `body_ip` to its back edge is now recognized under the same body rules with the var, the end and the step as int cells (the var stepped in place, its landing the compound one). The copy has no head compare (the head slot is a placeholder, which keeps the copy one instruction per original for the miss path's ip arithmetic) and its back edge is `op_c_cfor_back`: the step on the var cell, the compare against the end cell or literal, then the body again or the exit with the counted state popped; a step that overflows misses onto the general back edge, which raises its own text. The original back edge becomes `op_cells_cfor_back`: the general step and compare through the counted state's cached pointers (`cfor_advance`, shared with `op_cfor_back`), then the block entry for the next iteration; the entry (`cells_enter`) and the block run (`cells_run`) are one code path for both loop forms. A store value with no other use in the loop is a cell of adopted kind (the entry takes the slot's int or float; a string refuses the block), which is what a clear loop's `PIX[i] = color` needs. Both env safe-lists gain the cell opcodes of rows 112 to 115 (the classification runs before the lowering, so this is hygiene). Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 114 binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout only, hashes held): JaiDOOM 3.305 -> 3.26 ms/tic (-1.36%, paired -0.61 / -2.38 / +0.00 / -1.21 / -0.91 / -2.40), GLOOM serial 1.2269 -> 1.2537 ms/tick (+2.19%, paired +3.09 / +0.91 / +2.16 / +1.21 / +2.93 / -1.83), workers 8 0.9629 -> 0.9482 ms/tick (-1.53%, paired -2.69 / +3.38 / -7.38 / +4.72); this build also carries row 114b's per-iteration guard check. GLOOM's dispatched count is within 0.2% of row 114's (52,485,449 -> 52,372,787: one small counted loop closes) and its cell counts barely move, so the serial reading is the layout pattern rows 107, 111 and 113 showed with identical censuses (the main dispatch gains two cases) and the two-layout pair is the arbiter; the JaiDOOM gain is smaller than the VMPROF census suggested because that build inflates every dispatch. Census: JaiDOOM per timedemo: cell entries 1,967,741 -> 1,971,757 (4,017 counted entries: pix_clear's 3,984 calls and a few others), cell iterations 29,715,240 -> 66,997,512, misses 32 (loads), refusals 0; dispatched instructions 888,402,056 -> 813,829,512 (-8.4%: CFOR_BACK -37.3M, INDEX_STORE_FUSED -37.3M, the clear loop's 37.3M iterations run as a block); pix_clear exclusive 3,087 -> 1,006 Mcyc (5.05% -> 1.73% of the VMPROF timedemo); GLOOM per 600 ticks: dispatched 52,485,449 -> 52,372,787 (-0.2%: CFOR_BACK -24,016, INDEX_STORE_FUSED -26,136, one small counted loop closes), cell entries 87,594 -> 91,716, cell iterations 3,253,718 -> 3,269,490. | 116a | **Cell admission is a translation into canonical operations over admitted values, with named refusals, a legality and lifetime pass and an explicit origin table (Dev's ruling: the shape whitelist was the bug; design `out/fable/design_row116_cell_ir.md` v2 after Astra's architectural review).** The recognizer of rows 98 to 115 admitted bytecode shapes one at a time, so a loop's closing depended on which fusion the compiler chose for its spelling, and its invariants (temps reused per statement, guard slots shared by sites) were discovered by failures. The body of a loop now translates, once per chunk after the typed lowering, into `arith`, `mov`, `cmp` with its test, `load`, `store`, `math`, `jump`, `guard` and the back edge over semantic locals, compiler values, environment values, container bindings, snapshots and literals; the landing contract belongs to the operation (a stamped decl lands its declared kind, a store the slot's kind, a compiler value any kind, everything else a miss onto the general form). A legality pass follows: a container binding's slot is never written in the body, a compiler value is defined before it is read inside its statement, a snapshot's slot is written only by its own guard between the guard and its read. A refusal records its class and first offending instruction (`representation`, `effect`, `translation` for a supported core operation in an encoding not normalized yet, `malformed`, `head`); the profile prints closed and open loops per function and the disassembly lists them under each chunk. Every copy instruction carries its origin (`cell_loop::origin`) and every miss resumes through that table, not by position arithmetic. New coverage the translation gives for free: compare-jumps with run-time kinds at the head and inside (`op_c_cmp_any`, `op_c_jcmp_any`: JaiDOOM's `draw_patch` inner loop, `py >= VH`; `while (x < LIMIT)` with a `var` global), general element reads (`op_index_fused_decl` with an environment value in the index or an unproven container) as loads through a site the translation builds, and an environment value as a store's value. Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 115 binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout only, hashes held): second run, with the origin lookup on the miss path: GLOOM serial 1.2397 -> 1.2366 ms/tick (-0.24%, paired +1.45 / -0.92 / -0.07 / -1.26 / +0.64 / -1.46), JaiDOOM 3.275 -> 3.22 ms/tic (-1.68%, paired -1.52 / -1.53 / -1.83 / -1.53 / -0.61 / -1.83), workers 8 0.9359 -> 0.9319 (-0.42%, paired -7.44 / +2.41 / +1.50 / +1.66); the first run, with the origin table read at the top of every cell instruction, read JaiDOOM +1.07% (paired +1.54 / +0.00 / +0.00 / +1.84 / +0.31 / +1.53) and GLOOM +0.28% against the same frozen binaries, so the lookup now happens only on a miss; the censuses are identical to row 115 on both programs, so the translation is neutral by construction and the readings are placement and noise. Census: JaiDOOM and GLOOM instruction and cell censuses identical to row 115 (813,829,512 and 52,372,787 dispatched; cell entries 1,971,757 and 91,716; the same misses); the translation table: JaiDOOM 9 loops closed, 125 open (head 34, representation 20, effect 31, translation 40), GLOOM 6 closed, 68 open (head 26, representation 13, effect 8, translation 21); the hot open loops (draw_patch, draw_sprite, draw_psprite, trace_block_dz) all stop at a comparison value followed by JUMP_IF_TRUE, the || and && chains, named for row 116b. Controls (suite `Cell Loops`, 3 new): the translation-equivalence matrix (one sum in five spellings: `++i`, `i += 1`, `i = i + 1`, an identifier-start `for` and a literal-start `for`; all five close and land the same on both backends), the named refusals (a call and a `break` are effects, a string append is a translation gap, a member access is a representation), and the run-time-kind compares (the patch loop with `var` globals in its compares and store, an env-headed `while` on int, float and string limits); every earlier control kept, including both row 114 guard-slot regressions. | 116b | **The translation gaps the refusal table ranked first: a comparison value and the jump that consumes it (the `\|\|` and `&&` chains), a loop head that computes before its compare, the float family of `math`, and the untyped compound and increment statements.** After 116a the open-loop table put JaiDOOM's hot open loops (`draw_patch`, `draw_sprite`, `draw_psprite`, `trace_block_dz`) on one encoding: `if (a < 0 \|\| a >= h)` compiles as a comparison pushed as a value followed by `JUMP_IF_TRUE`. That pair is now one `cmp` whose target word carries the jump's sense (never an inverted operator, since a NaN fails both `<` and `>=`); the pair's origin is the value instruction (a miss re-runs it whole and the general jump follows, so no stack entry is owed) and the jump's copy is a placeholder the verifier checks no branch targets. A compare-headed loop's head is the span from the back edge's target to the compare: the condition's temps, environment reads and element reads translate like the body and run every iteration before the compare (`while (i < n * 2)`, `while (i < LIMIT + n)`, `while (i < b[n])`), and the compare's miss resumes at its own origin. `math` covers the pure one-argument intrinsics (`itrunc` and `ifloor` land an int, `abs` keeps its argument's kind and misses onto the kernel's overflow text at the minimum int, `floor`, `ceil`, `sqrt`, `sin`, `cos`, `tan` and the angle conversions land a float with the kernel's constants). `x op= y` and `++x` / `x--` in statement position on a slot the typed lowering left general (a `var`, a float literal) are `arith` with the compound landing and kinds at run time. Two more rules came out of the probe: a guarded container that is a reference to a map entry (JaiDOOM's sprite table is `var SPR_PIX = {}`, so `var& tp = SPR_PIX[lump]` is a map entry, not an element) is admitted like a pinned table entry, since no cell store writes a map; and a container site that refuses the entry marks the loop in the call record's cell state so the entry is not attempted again in that call (the refusal valve): before the map admission the patch loop closed but was refused at every back edge, 7.19M refused entries per timedemo paying the guards for nothing, and `draw_patch` got slower. Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 116a binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout only, hashes held): JaiDOOM 3.17 -> 3.04 ms/tic (-4.10%, paired -4.09 / -3.15 / -3.80 / -4.10 / -4.40 / -4.10), GLOOM serial 1.2104 -> 1.2082 ms/tick (-0.18%, paired -0.14 / -1.22 / +0.09 / -0.29 / +0.99 / -0.23), workers 8 0.7737 -> 0.7798 ms/tick (+0.79%, paired +0.33 / +1.53 / -2.63 / +2.32); GLOOM's census is identical to 116a's (its one new closure is cold), so its readings are the run noise. Census: JaiDOOM per timedemo: loops closed 9 -> 12 (draw_patch's inner loop among them), cell entries 1,971,757 -> 2,785,509 (one per patch column), cell iterations 66,997,512 -> 73,378,024, misses 32, refusals 0; dispatched instructions 813,829,512 -> 720,778,224 (-11.4%: T_ADD_II -14.4M, T_LOAD -14.2M, CFOR_BACK -8.0M, FUSED_CMP_JUMP, OPERAND_TEMP, T_DIV_II, T_JGE_II and T_MUL_II -7.2M each: the patch loop's 7.19M iterations run as a block); draw_patch leaves the top five functions (6,360 -> 2,648 Mcyc in the VMPROF build); the open-loop table: 122 open (head 24, representation 23, effect 34, translation 41), the remaining hot open loops (draw_sprite, draw_psprite, trace_block_dz, vid_row_half) stopping at stack forms, the virtual-stack item; GLOOM per 600 ticks: dispatched identical (52,372,787), loops closed 6 -> 7, open 67 (head 17, representation 14, effect 8, translation 28). Controls (suite `Cell Loops`, 1 new and 1 extended): the patch loop keeps JaiDOOM's `\|\|` spelling and closes together with the env-headed `while` and the `\|\|` chain loop; head spans (a temp, an environment value, an element read in the condition), the math family on int and float values with the `abs` overflow text, and `+= 2`, `+= 0.5`, `-= 3`, `*= 2`, `%= 1000`, `++i`, `i++` on `var` slots (a kind change lands through the general conversion) land the same on both backends with proofs on and off, and every one of those nine loops closes.
