| 117 | **A local's landing follows its declared type, which is the language's own rule (design note section 9): `var` and `auto` locals become cells, typed locals keep the pinned forms, and the run-time judgment happens only where the language itself decides at run time.** The entry admitted a local only when its slot carried this engine's canonical int or float stamp, so a `var` local never became a cell (its slot carries the `any` stamp) and every landing was judged against the slot's current kind alone. The entry now records the stamp of each adopted-kind cell (the canonical int or float of an inferred `auto` or unproven local, the `any` stamp of a `var`) and the flush restores it; a pinned cell needs none, its slot carries the canonical stamp and its landing is the declared kind. The landing rule per operation: an int slot lands ints (a float result misses onto the checked conversion), a float slot lands floats and widens ints by a declaration or a store alike, and an `any` slot lands whatever kind the operation produced. The translation admits `var` and `auto` declarations as cells of adopted kind; an element read lands in place for a pinned-kind or compiler-value destination (`op_c_load`, the site's expected kind is the cell's kind by construction) and through the declared-type rule for an adopted-kind destination (`op_c_load_any`), the same split the arithmetic has. Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 116b binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout, hashes held): GLOOM serial 1.2077 -> 1.2073 ms/tick (-0.03%, paired -0.71 / -0.66 / +0.23 / +0.92 / +1.29 / -1.14), JaiDOOM 3.055 -> 3.06 ms/tic (+0.16%, paired 0.00 / +0.66 / -0.33 / +0.33 / 0.00 / +0.33), workers 8 0.8642 -> 0.8969 ms/tick (+3.78%, paired +4.66 / +3.12 / +3.99 / +2.94; that workload read 0.78 to 0.91 ms/tick across the evening's pairs and its A/A' control spanned ten points, so the reading is open, neither credited nor debited). The row's cost was measured down over four builds against the same frozen binaries: the first landed every element read through `cell_land` (an out-of-line function) and read JaiDOOM +0.33%; the second restored the direct landing behind a kind compare on every read, +0.98%; the third recorded the stamp for adopted-kind cells only, +1.31%; an A/A' control (a fresh build of the 116b source, six PE-header bytes apart from the frozen one) read +0.16% with mixed signs, and a second layout (both sources rebuilt with `vm_backend.cpp` in another code section via `#pragma code_seg`, 1.57 MB of the image moved) read the third build at +0.98%, so the one percent was code, not placement: the kind compare on every element read, which the load split removes for the pinned case, and the fourth build reads the A/A' floor. GLOOM serial across those pairs read +1.76 / +1.99 / +0.61 / -0.88 (A/A') / +0.47% with paired spans of two to nine points and the frozen baseline itself drifting 2.5% between pairs, so it resolves nothing at this size. Census: JaiDOOM per timedemo dispatched instructions 720,778,224 -> 720,778,224 (identical), loops closed 12, cell entries 2,785,509, iterations 73,378,024, misses 32, refused entries 0; the open-loop table 122 open (head 24, representation 25, effect 36, translation 37; against 116b's 24 / 23 / 34 / 41, four `var` loops now translate further in and stop at stack forms); GLOOM per 600 ticks dispatched 52,372,787 (identical), loops closed 7, open 67 (head 17, representation 16, effect 8, translation 26); VMPROF totals against 116b +0.54% (JaiDOOM) and +0.45% (GLOOM) on identical op counts, inside that instrument's build-to-build spread. Controls (suite `Cell Loops`, 1 new): a `var` accumulator over ints and over a float table (the kind change lands and the `any` stamp stays), an `auto` accumulator, an int local fed a float (the general form's checked conversion), a float local fed an int through a store (widened), a `var` landing a float element, all landing the same on both backends with proofs on and off; the four value-model loops close, and the two stack pairs (an element read stored into a widening float, an element read stored into a `var`) are named as gaps for the canonical lowering. | 118a | **Compiler values are definitions, not slots (`design_row118_canonical_lowering.md` section 4, the second of Astra's four revisions of the cell model).** The compiler reuses a temp slot across statements, and the translation keyed cells by slot, so an int element read and a float subtraction landing in the same temp were two kinds on one local and the loop was refused with the representation class; `draw_seg`'s 260-instruction column loop, refused at its `T_DIV_FF`, is that case (`done[x]` lands an int in the first temp, `(x - x1f) / spanx` lands a float in it two statements later). A temp write whose kind differs from the slot's current definition now opens a new cell for the same slot; reads bind to the latest definition in program order; the emission advances a per-slot binding as it passes each definition (an instruction's destination is its own definition, its sources are the definitions before it); the copy names cells directly, in its instruction words, in its own site rows at the copy instruction's index (the general row keeps its slots) and in cloned store protos (index and value operands as cells, the container the general operand resolved at entry), so `slot_to_cell` leaves the run time. A definition is live from the instruction after it through its last read, recorded per copy instruction, and the flush writes a slot only from a live definition, so a dead value never overwrites the one the general continuation reads (the control's overflow case: the first statement's int add misses at `INT64_MAX` while the second statement's float definition of the same temp is dirty from the previous iteration). Semantic locals keep one cell per slot: two kinds on a declared local remain the general form's conversion. Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 117 binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout, hashes held): GLOOM serial 1.2116 -> 1.2147 ms/tick (+0.26%, paired -0.81 / -0.42 / +0.56 / +0.63 / -1.79 / +0.48), JaiDOOM 3.075 -> 3.095 ms/tic (+0.65%, paired +0.98 / -1.29 / +0.65 / -1.27 / +1.31 / -0.65, three of six positive with alternating signs, the A/A' floor's pattern), workers 8 0.8769 -> 0.8933 ms/tick (+1.87%, paired -1.79 / +0.71 / -0.44 / +7.07); the frozen baseline back at its earlier readings (1.2116 and 3.075 against 1.2077 and 3.055 an hour before the drift). Two earlier pairs of the same source without the last trim, on a box that had drifted 7% slow (the frozen 117 binaries themselves read GLOOM 1.30 and JaiDOOM 3.22 against 1.21 and 3.05 an hour earlier; the first of the two, taken while desktop applications loaded the box, is void), read GLOOM serial +2.4% and JaiDOOM +1.1% with five of six pairs positive on both: the copy's operand resolution had gained a bounds check against the cell vector's `size()` per index operand, a division by the 24-byte cell size on every cell load and store operand, where the old lookup had a constant compare; the emission builds those rows, so the range holds by construction and the landed build has the constant compare back. Census: JaiDOOM per timedemo dispatched instructions 720,778,224 -> 720,778,224 (identical), loops closed 12, cell entries 2,785,509, iterations 73,378,024, misses 32; the open-loop table 122 open (head 24, representation 23, effect 38, translation 37; against 117's 24 / 25 / 36 / 37, draw_seg's column loop and monster_brain's loop at line 876 leave the representation class for their first call); GLOOM per 600 ticks dispatched 52,372,787 (identical), loops closed 7, open 67 (head 17, representation 16, effect 8, translation 26); VMPROF totals against 117 -0.30% (GLOOM) and +0.09% (JaiDOOM) on identical op counts. Controls (suite `Cell Loops`, 1 new): `draw_seg`'s spelling (an element test with `continue`, a float subtraction divided into a float declaration, a float accumulation over a typed table) and a `while` loop whose first statement lands an int through a `var&` read and whose second lands a float through a typed table, both closing; an int overflow in the first statement after the float definition of the previous iteration reporting the overflow, on both backends, proofs on and off. | 118b | **The move rows are the general move (`design_row118_canonical_lowering.md` section 5; the first increment of the canonical scalar lowering: meaning-equivalent spellings compile to one shape).** `op_t_store_mov`, `op_t_store_const`, `op_t_decl_mov` and `op_t_decl_const` carry a total run-time guard (the same-type payload test on the live slot lands in place, anything else replays the exact general pair from the typed site), so a same-type proof added nothing to their soundness, only to their emission condition; `try_lower_store_move` and `try_lower_decl_move` now require neither the proofs flag nor the same-type proof (statement position, a value slot, no reference alias and no `::name` as before), so `x = y` and `T x = y` from a slot or a literal are one row whatever the checker proved, with proofs on or off, and `x = a[i];` in statement position lands its read in a compiler temp (the `T_LOAD` row) and moves it with the same row. The cell translation already had the arms for these rows, so the 117 control's two stack pairs close; the proofs flag now gates the arithmetic and compare rows only. | 116b.1 | **Admission is non-observing (row 116b follow-up, Astra's finding of 2026-09-11).** The block entry admitted a guarded reference to a map entry by the holder's shape and then dereferenced it before any loop code ran, so a key erased since the reference was taken threw `Reference to a removed map entry` at admission on a path the general form never takes (Astra's `removed_map_untaken.jai`: the interpreter and the VM without proofs print 0 then 3, the VM with proofs caught the text twice; rows 114b and earlier have no map-entry arm). The admission now requires the key to be present through the holder's non-throwing lookup (the `find` that `resolve_target` uses); a stale entry refuses the block, the refusal valve keeps it refused for the call, and the general form raises only where the loop itself reads the entry. The rule behind it, now in the design: a missing or dead binding refuses the block and never raises, for every binding kind the entry resolves. Census: JaiDOOM per timedemo dispatched instructions 719,897,965 (identical to 118b), cell entries 2,785,509, refused 0, loops closed 12; GLOOM per 600 ticks dispatched 52,311,909 (identical), loops closed 7: the map-entry loop of JaiDOOM (draw_patch's) keeps its key, so nothing refuses. | 118c | **A unary over an operand is a definition into a compiler value (`design_row118_canonical_lowering.md` section 5, the second increment of the canonical scalar lowering).** `op_unary_temp`, the shape of `op_math_temp`, lands a unary minus, complement or not in a compiler temp from an operand (a slot, a literal, an environment scalar, an element read, a math call, another unary, or a binary that lands fused, each into its own temp first), and the consumers take it as an operand: the lift and the flat pre-step (a unary is an operand shape that needs a quiet partner, like a math call), a math argument, a declaration (`T v = -e` lands the unary in a temp and moves it with the move row), a statement-position store and an element store's value; the general unary runs verbatim underneath the row (its texts, its bound decode, its truthiness), so the row is emitted with proofs on or off. The cell translation lands neg and bnot in the copy (`op_c_neg` through the checked negation, `INT64_MIN` a miss onto the kernel's overflow text; `op_c_bnot` on an int), and `!` stays a representation refusal (a bool is a kind the model does not take yet). The listing prints every row's operands (Astra's finding: the typed rows printed none, so `x = x + 1` and `x = x + 2` listed identically). A language fix rides along, found by the row's control: `-a[i]` and `~a[i]` on an element read raised `Unary minus requires numeric operand` on both backends (an element read arrives as a reference wrapper, the rhs-lvalue shape, and neither unary dereferenced it, unlike every other consumer; the fuzz never flagged it because both backends agreed); the unary row dereferences its operand, which made the VM compute the value while the interpreter kept raising, and both unaries now consume the value, byte-parallel. | 118e | **A negated numeric literal is a literal, and a counted loop takes an environment bound (two canonicalizations the post-118d open-loop table named).** The parser produced a unary minus over a literal, so `-1` cost an operation per evaluation, `x != -1` never took the fused compare, `int other = -1` never took the constant row, and `cross_check`'s `while (bm[i] != -1)` head stayed open; the parser now folds `-<int literal>` and `-<float literal>` into the literal at the prefix-unary site (the lexer's largest magnitude is `INT64_MAX`, so the negation always fits; `-0.0` keeps IEEE's sign as the runtime negation would; `-x` on a variable keeps the unary), which both backends consume as the one AST, and the `.jaibite` version is 17 (the serialization suite's version pin moves with it). A counted loop whose bound or step is an environment scalar (`render_view`'s `for (int i = 0; i < VW; ++i)`, `wad_load`'s) now enters the block: the general form reads the global through a pointer at every back edge and no cell instruction writes a global, so the environment cell read once at entry holds the same value. Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 118d binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout, hashes held): GLOOM serial 1.1979 -> 1.2037 ms/tick (+0.48%, paired -1.24 / +0.34 / +0.25 / -0.75 / +1.11 / +0.85, mixed), JaiDOOM 2.855 -> 2.85 ms/tic (-0.18%, paired 0.00 / 0.00 / -0.70 / -1.05 / +0.35 / 0.00, mixed), workers 8 0.8884 -> 0.8422 ms/tick (-5.20%, paired -12.30 / +9.65 / -9.65 / +1.69, noise); neutral within the floor, a canonicalization row under the simplification rule. | 119.1 | **The interpreter's loop fast paths re-resolve their frame pointers after every body run, and a lambda's frame reserves its slots (fix; row 119a's control found it).** The counted-for fast path cached raw pointers to the counter, the bound and the step, and the four range-for loops cached the loop variable's pointer, all into the frame's locals vector, across the body; a lambda's function object was minted without its `local_count` (a namespace function's too), so the frame reserved nothing and the locals grew by push_back as the body declared, reallocating under those pointers (AddressSanitizer: heap-use-after-free, a read at `visit_for_stmt`'s post-body kind guard and a write at `visit_range_for_stmt`'s element binding). Debug's freed fill trips the kind guard into the general continuation with the right result; Release reads stale bytes: a lambda with a counted loop, a `continue` and two callees of different frame sizes ran one iteration (184 read as 41), a range loop read a stale element (247 read as 246). Named functions reserve their slots and never grew, so the timedemo and GLOOM hashes never moved and the fuzz never saw it. The pointers are re-resolved after every body run and the lambda and namespace mints pass `local_count`. Controls (suite `Loop Frame Storage`, 1 new): the counted loop with a continue and two calls, the range loop with the same body, a variable bound and step, a string range loop, all landing the same value on both backends. Gates (with row 119a on the same tree): Debug 3082 x2, XTHREAD Parallel 119 x2, Release 3110 x2, fuzz 3000 + 1500 `--no-proofs`: 0 new divergences, 0 crashes (both chains), GLOOM 300-tick hashes held (workers 0 and 8) and 600-tick held, timedemo 2.88 ms/tic. | this session | Debug, XTHREAD, Release suites both backends, fuzz, hashes | gated with 119a, held for landing | (this commit) |
| 119a | **A call inside a cell block (design note section 9, the fourth increment of the canonical scalar lowering; JaiDOOM's open-loop table had 29 loops stopping at a call, `draw_seg` first).** The probe, the argument zone, the call and its consumer translate as one operation of the body. An argument is a value: a cell boxed at the call (a slot's cell is flushed first, so a by-reference parameter binds the current value), a constant or an environment name pushed by the general instruction run verbatim, or a computed argument (a fused binary, an element read, a math call or a unary over a slot) landing in a compiler value of its own ahead of the call, with the probe as its origin. The probe, the pushes and the call run the general executors verbatim over three copy instructions at the call's place (`C_PROBE`, `C_ARGS`, `C_CALL`; the pushes' ip is an argument zone of its own), the callee runs in the dispatcher with the caller parked on the call's copy, and the landing (`C_LAND`) resumes the block. The call is the effect point: a slot argument's kind is checked after it (a by-reference parameter boxes the caller's slot, so the local is the slot's from then on), the environment values are re-read, the container sites re-resolved, and any change misses at the consumer with the result on the stack; the result lands by the declared-type rule into a scalar declaration or a no-result store, or a statement's is dropped; a callee that raises into the caller's handler flushes the block first. The copy is no longer one instruction per original: the emission maps positions (a boxed argument and a carried jump have no copy instruction, a call has four). The door's instructions live off the block loop's hot switch in a cold helper (the first build had them in the switch and the pair read JaiDOOM +1.75% with the census down 0.44%: the block loop's code generation, not the door), a statement call never pushes its result, and the entry and the landing resolve container sites only when the loop has any. Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 118e binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout, hashes held): GLOOM serial 1.1947 -> 1.2101 ms/tick (+1.29%, paired -1.10 / +1.91 / -2.07 / +0.92 / +1.23 / +0.79, mixed, band: GLOOM has no call inside a block and its census is unchanged), JaiDOOM 2.86 -> 2.855 ms/tic (-0.18%, paired +0.71 / -0.35 / 0.00 / 0.00 / -0.35 / +0.70, neutral), workers 8 0.8686 -> 0.8657 ms/tick (-0.33%, paired -1.27 / +1.23 / +1.95 / -5.31, noise); neutral within the floor: the door costs nothing measurable (a 3M-call micro against the general form: +14 ns per statement call, +2 ns per declaration call) and the credit is in the loops it opens. Census: JaiDOOM per timedemo dispatched instructions 655,921,241 -> 653,064,725 (-2,856,516; -0.44%): PROBE_CALLEE and CALL_FROM_SCRATCH 7,871,340 -> 7,351,576 (-519,764 each, the calls now made from inside blocks; C_LAND 519,764 dispatched, landings missed 0), LOOP_BACK 7,432,499 -> 6,659,289 and T_JLT_II 11,838,656 -> 11,065,446 (-773,210 each, the heads of the loops that now run as blocks), LOAD -523,988, T_ADD_II -517,400, CELLS_BACK 2,156,028 -> 2,411,838 (+255,810: bsp_subsector's loop over draw_seg, which calls it from a block now); loops closed 18 -> 23 (bsp_subsector, barrel_explode, demo_state_hash's second loop, tex_warm_map's two), 22 loops pass their calls and 17 of them stop at the next door: draw_seg at REMAIN = REMAIN - 1 (a global scalar store), pos_ok_scan's three, tag_floor, tag_plats and monster_brain at a call result used as a condition, draw_sprite's fuzz loop at a call result stored into an element, tex_masked at a nested counted loop, mover_run's two at a return, snd_sector and demo_state_hash at an element read pushed as an argument; the open-loop table 116 -> 111 (head 15, representation 24, effect 49 -> 33, translation 28 -> 39); cell entries 2,979,509 -> 3,235,322, refused 4,890 -> 4,898, cell iterations 76,967,003 -> 77,485,346; GLOOM per 600 ticks unchanged (52,143,141 dispatched, loops closed 7 -> 8, a cold loop); VMPROF totals against 118e -0.6% (JaiDOOM, 49,023 -> 48,707 Mcyc) and -2.3% (GLOOM), within the build-to-build variance. Controls (suite `Cell Loops`, 1 new): `draw_seg`'s shape, a callee writing a global the loop reads, a callee growing an array the loop reads, an `int&` taken on a loop local, a float result into an int declaration and its rejection, a throw out of the callee into the caller's catch, a recursive callee, a zero-argument callee, a string result into a `var`, and a negation, a math call, an element read and an environment name as argument values, all landing the same on both backends with proofs on and off, every one of the eleven loops closing with one `C_CALL` per call. | 119b | **An environment scalar written inside a block (design note section 9, the fifth increment of the canonical scalar lowering; `draw_seg` stopped at `REMAIN = REMAIN - 1` after row 119a).** An environment cell (a global, or the member a bare name resolves to) is no longer read-only: a fused store, a compound, an increment, a move or a call result landing on a global writes the same environment cell its reads share, by the declared-type rule against the stamp read at entry (an int global takes ints, a float global widens an int, a `var` lands any kind), and the flush writes the payload back through the same resolution the reads use, in place when the storage still holds the kind and stamp read at entry, else a fresh value with that stamp. A call is the effect point: dirty environment cells are flushed ahead of the probe and re-read after the return; `::name` stores stay general. Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 119a binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout, hashes held): GLOOM serial 1.2045 -> 1.1947 ms/tick (-0.82%, paired -0.93 / -12.24 / -0.93 / +0.32 / -0.50 / -0.69, one outlier, band), JaiDOOM 2.845 -> 2.84 ms/tic (-0.18%, paired -0.35 / 0.00 / +2.82 / -0.35 / -0.35 / -0.35, neutral), workers 8 0.8171 -> 0.8361 ms/tick (+2.33%, paired -4.19 / +2.27 / +4.63 / +1.12, noise); neutral: no hot loop closes yet (draw_seg needs the compare door and bool cells), a canonicalization row under the simplification rule. Controls (suite `Cell Loops`, 1 new): a global counted down by a fused store, stepped by a compound and an increment, read by the loop's head, read by a callee after the block wrote it, rewritten by a callee, a `var` global changing kind, an int global fed a float, a float global fed ints, a throw after the write, moves of a slot and a constant onto globals, and a call result into a global, all landing the same on both backends with proofs on and off, every one of the eleven loops closing. | 119d | **Bool is a cell kind (design note sections 3 and 9, the seventh increment of the canonical scalar lowering; `draw_seg` tests `solid`, `fsky && bsky` and `!solid` inside its column loop).** A bool local, environment value or compiler value holds its payload in a cell beside the int and float kinds (payload 0 or 1, the canonical bool stamp), enters, lands and flushes like them, and a bool cell takes bools only (an int declaration fed a bool, or a bool fed an int, is the general conversion). The truth test is an operation of its own (the `!` token on the compare ops): the language's truthiness of an int, a float or a bool, on a local or an environment value, as a head (`while (RUNNING)`) or in a body (`if (solid)`, `if (!x)` through the jump's sense). `!x` as a value inverts that truthiness into a bool (`C_LNOT`), a comparison as a value runs the compare kernel and lands a bool (`C_CMPVAL`), and a general move the typed rows left alone (a bool constant declared or stored, a slot copied) is one move by the declared-type rule; a bool argument boxes and a bool call result lands. | 119e | **A block holds up to 128 cells, a local the body declares enters empty, and a landing after a call re-validates in proportion to what the call could have changed (the eighth increment of the canonical scalar lowering; `draw_seg`'s column loop, 21.6% of JaiDOOM's exclusive time, translated to its end after rows 119a to 119d and was refused as too many cells, then at every back edge by three slots holding null, then cost more as a block than as the general form).** The dirty set, the compiler-value set, the environment-value set and the per-instruction live sets were one 64-bit word each; the masks are a two-word set now (`cell_mask`) and the limit is 128, a mechanical change at every mask site. Then the entry guard, which demanded every cell's slot hold the cell's kind, refused the loop 1.84 million times per timedemo at locals declared inside branches the general form had not taken (`c1`, `w0`, `w1`: declared in the body, re-assigned in a branch, their slots holding a null placeholder): a cell whose first write in the loop is its declaration (`first_decl`, its stamp kept as `decl_stamp`) needs no entry kind and no unbox, starts empty, and its declaration lands by the declared kind (an int declaration takes ints, a float declaration widens an int, a `var` or `auto` declaration takes any kind); the flush stamps the value with the declaration's type. With the loop entering, the first pair read JaiDOOM +3.86% (6 of 6) while its dispatches fell 19%: the landing after a call re-read every environment cell through the operand resolver and re-resolved every container site by scanning the whole copy, about 1,700 cycles for a loop of some 80 cells and 16 sites, four times per column. The landing's cost model is the third change: the copy's sites are a list (`cell_loop::sites`), the entry records how each site's container is reached (its frame slot, or the environment binding the lookup cache answered, valid while the environment and its epoch hold) and each environment cell's binding under the same condition, and a landing whose environment and epoch still hold re-reads the environment cells through their bindings and checks each site's container by identity (the same array node behind the slot or binding, a plain-cell reference dereferenced), resolving in full only on a difference and missing only if that fails. The census names the cells whose entry refused; the listing prints each closed loop's cells. Measured (micro, 3M iterations, a statement call per iteration, the landing as the call loop's time less the same loop without the call): a statement call from the block 91 ns either way; the landing with 6 sites 31 -> 16 ns, with 12 sites 62 -> 43 ns, with 8 environment cells 32 -> 14 ns. | 119f | **An exit ends a block (the ninth increment of the canonical scalar lowering; a return inside the loop, a break that leaves it and a compare whose taken edge leaves it were refused as effects, which kept draw_sprite's and draw_masked's column loops (a break), draw_things' two clip loops, cross_check's inner loop, lump_num_from's and name8's scans (a compare's edge out) and mover_busy's search (a return) on the general form).** An exit statement, a `return` or a `break` whose jump leaves the loop, ends the block at the statement's first instruction (the statement's instructions are found through the chunk's statement table, so a return with any expression, a break behind its region exits and scope pops, and a break out of a switch inside the loop, which stays a jump, are told apart without a shape); the cells flush and the general form runs the statement whole, so nothing of it needs a copy, and a jump to its first instruction lands on the exit. A compare whose taken edge leaves the loop lands on an exit appended after the copy's back edge with the general target as its operand (`C_EXIT`), one per target. | 120 | **The region verifier runs over the control flow graph, and the listing prints each region's canonical operations (the first row of the middle end; Dev's direction of 2026-09-11: correctness first, then optimization in the LLVM manner transcoded into C++; plan `out/fable/plan_middle_end.md`).** The translation's legality check held that a compiler value is defined earlier in program order than it is read, sound for the shapes admitted today but not the rule a pass may rely on; the check is now the must-define dataflow over the region's control flow graph (successors: the next operation, a compare's target or the exit, a jump's target, nothing after an exit, the back edge re-entering at the first operation), so a slot the block enters with undefined must be defined on every path to each of its reads, and a definition in an untaken branch does not reach a read after the join; a failing region is refused as malformed. With `ir_listing` on (the listing tool, the tests; nested compilers inherit it) each closed region records its operations as text and the disassembly prints a `-- ir` line under its cells line. No pass yet and no run-time change: measured neutral. | 121a | **The middle end's switch, and the first proof-based elision: pinned cells skip their per-read kind checks (row 121a of `out/fable/plan_middle_end.md`).** `engine::ir_passes(bool)` is the switch over every proof-based elision and pass of the region compiler; off, a region runs its checked canonical form, observationally identical, and `jaiscript_fuzz --no-passes` is its differential gate (a third chain beside proofs on and `--no-proofs`). Behind it: a pinned cell (a declared int, float or bool) holds its kind for the whole block, since every write lands that kind or misses and the region verifier proves no read ahead of a first declaration on any path; an arithmetic or compare in a pinned typed row whose operand cells are pinned to the row's kind (constants of that kind included) is emitted in its unchecked form (`C_ARITH_P`, `C_CMP_P`, `C_JCMP_P`, the same handler with the checks folded away), an adopted kind keeps the checked form, every landing keeps its rule. VMPROF counts cell instructions by opcode. Measured (micro, 3M iterations per shape, before and after): one arith per iteration 5.67 -> 5.67 ns, three arith 10.67 -> 11.0, a diamond 11.0 -> 11.0, element reads 9.67 -> 10.0, element stores 16.3 -> 16.3, float arith 8.0 -> 7.67, two moves 9.67 -> 10.3 (ABBA x2; the differences are within the run spread, and a cell operation costs about 2.8 ns in this build, dispatch and bookkeeping rather than the checks). Measured (Fable's pair: frozen row 120 binaries against this build, ABBA alternating, medians, normal layout, hashes held): GLOOM serial 1.2016 -> 1.2035 ms/tick (+0.16%, band; paired +1.70 / +0.95 / +0.35 / +1.05 / +0.41 / -1.62), JaiDOOM 2.795 -> 2.785 ms/tic (-0.36%, band; paired 0.00 / -0.36 / 0.00 / +1.07 / 0.00 / -1.42), workers 8 0.8684 -> 0.8706 ms/tick (+0.26%, band; paired +1.97 / -6.32 / +4.11 / +1.80): neutral, the per-read kind checks were predicted branches and cost nothing measurable. | 122 | **Strings are a cell kind: a block borrows a string's node and appends in place (`out/fable/plan_row122_string_cells.md`; the per-opcode census after 121a put 19.9% of JaiDOOM's time in `vid_row_half`, a string-building loop of 180M general instructions per timedemo refused at its string element read).** A cell of kind string holds a borrowed node pointer, never a handle; ownership stays in the slot, the constant, the environment binding or the container. A slot holding a string enters as a string cell (adopted for a `var`, pinned for a declared `string`; a parallel-borrowed or C++-bound string refuses), a string environment value is a cell, a string constant an operand, a string element loads into a statement temp from a typed `array<string>` buffer or a hetero array's string value. `C_APPEND`, the compound plus with run-time kinds, appends two strings in place when the destination's slot still holds the borrowed node uniquely and the memory cap accepts the bytes; any other case misses onto the general form (its unshare, char-as-text and mismatch texts stand), numeric kinds fall through to the any-kind arithmetic. String cells are never flushed; slot-backed ones are re-borrowed after every call. Both encodings of the loop translate (a pushing element read consumed by a compound plus on a slot, with the read as the pair's origin; a compound plus with an operand on a slot). | 118.1 | **A compound assignment reads its target after its right side, on both backends (fix; Astra's probe `slot_read_across_mutating_call.jai`, 2026-09-11, and its pre-boxed control).** With `mutate(int& v) { v = 20; return 2; }`, `x += mutate(x)` returned 22 on the VM and raised `Type mismatch` on the interpreter, on every build back to row 114b, while `x + mutate(x)` and `x = x + mutate(x)` gave 5 on both. The interpreter's identifier compound path took `script_value& target = varPtr->deref()` before evaluating the right side; a by-reference argument in the right side boxes the variable into a cell in place, so the saved reference then named the slot's reference wrapper and the raw-type ladder reported the mismatch. The general tail of that path now runs against the binding re-resolved after the right side (the fast paths for a literal or identifier right side run no user code and are unchanged), which is the language's order for a compound assignment (the right operand before the left, as C++17 sequences every assignment operator) and what the VM's compound store does by construction. The rule is recorded as invariant 12 (`docs/invariants.md`): never carry a C++ reference to a slot's current representation across an evaluation that can run user code; re-resolve the binding and read the payload where the language's order puts the read. Controls (new suite `Compound Assignment Order`, 1 test): `x + mutate(x)` and `x = x + mutate(x)` at 5, `x += mutate(x)` and `x -= mutate(x)` at 22 and 18, a pre-boxed target, the right side hoisted into a local, a `var` target, and the compound's own value, all the same on both backends. Gates: Debug 3077 x2, XTHREAD Parallel 119 x2, Release 3105 x2, fuzz 3000 + 1500 `--no-proofs`: 0 new divergences, 0 crashes (both chains), GLOOM 300-tick hashes held (workers 0 and 8) and 600-tick held, timedemo 1746772898 at 3.07 ms/tic. No pair and no census change: the change is in the interpreter's compound path. | this session (finding: Astra) | Debug, XTHREAD, Release suites both backends, fuzz, hashes | landed on the worktree (held stack, after 116b.1) | (this commit) |
| 118.1b | **The interpreter reads a compound assignment's reference target after its right-hand side (fix; Astra's backlog-drain finding, 2026-09-11 18:00).** `value += restore(data)` where `value` is a reference to a removed array element or map entry and the right side restores it: the interpreter dereferenced the target before the right side ran, to probe its fast paths, and raised the removed-element error with `calls = 0`, where the VM, whose right value is already on the stack, ran `restore()` first and stored 22. The language's order is the right side first: a reference that is not a plain cell (an element, a map entry, a property) is now dereferenced only after the right side, through the checked twin of `deref` (a raw throw there surfaced as the function's return value); a plain cell or a value slot cannot raise on a dereference, so the fast paths keep reading it first. | 119a.1 | **A block's callee probe runs first, at its own place; a getter it observes re-lands the block; a verbatim load among the arguments is an effect barrier for computed arguments (fix; Astra's third finding 2026-09-11 and its callee-getter follow-up).** The call door computed an argument's value ahead of the callee probe and of every verbatim push, while the general form resolves the callee first and pushes the arguments in source order, so a getter reached by the callee's resolution (`var& F = box.callable`) or by a verbatim push (`take(V, G + 1)` with V a reference to a property) ran after a value it should have preceded (the interpreter 93, the VM 73). The probe is a region operation of its own (`ir::probe`, `C_PROBE` at the probe's place); the callee ladder reports a resolution through a property and the block re-lands (`cells_reland`, shared with the call's landing) before any argument value runs; a verbatim environment load or a reference-slot load (`chunk::reference_slots`) is an effect barrier at translation and a computed argument after one refuses the loop; a miss between the probe and the pushes replays from the first argument instruction with the callee parked, so an observed getter never runs twice (`design_row118_canonical_lowering.md` section 9, the effect points and the four replay points). | 118d.1 | **An operand the language reads before a hoisted read keeps its place: binding identity, quietness and the value snapshot are three separate facts (fix; Astra's second finding 2026-09-11 with the review notes of 18:46, 18:59, 19:19 and 19:50).** The VM hoisted an inner read into a temp before the outer container or the left operand was read, so user code run by that read (an `operator[]`, a getter behind a reference as the index or the receiver, a class operator or a custom numeric operator inside the index, a callback rebinding what a reference parameter names) could rebind the outer binding first; the in-op subscript operand of a fused binary had the same exposure. A private local (a value declaration no reference or capture reaches) stays in place. A hoisted read is quiet only when its container is a private local, its element class is proven and its index is a literal, a private local or a quiet read; a unary or an intrinsic is quiet over a literal, a quiet read or a private local declared int, float or bool; a binary is never quiet (a custom numeric operator is user code the compiler cannot see); an environment name the unit does not declare as a value may be a reference to a property, so it reads first either way. Every other operand read ahead of an observing read is taken ahead of it: a container through the guard's always form (`op_operand_temp` c = 2, `fused_index_proto::guard_always`; the site stays keyed on the original operand and the block's `C_GUARD` restores the snapshot), a scalar through a value temp the region translates as a move; a reference container ahead of an observing inner read snapshots its value too. Controls (suite Cell Loops): twelve probes on both backends with proofs on and off (Astra's operator[], typed-getter, class-operator, custom-operator, reference-parameter, outer-reference and guard-reuse cases), a private local keeping the fused shape and its loop closed, a guarded container still closing as a block. | this session (finding: Astra, backlog drain 2026-09-11) | Debug 3097x2 (interpreter and vm), XTHREAD Parallel 119x2, Release 3125x2, fuzz 3000 + 1500 --no-proofs + 1500 --no-passes: 0 new divergences, 0 crashes (all three chains), GLOOM 300-tick hashes held (workers 0 and 8) and 600-tick held, timedemo 1746772898 at 3.23 ms/tic (the chain has no row 122); the census against row 121a: block entries and closed loops unchanged (JaiDOOM 3,625,421 entries, GLOOM 91,886), At the final stable tip 7fc75044: XTHREAD Parallel 119x2, Release 3127x2, fuzz 3000 + 1500 --no-proofs + 1500 --no-passes with 0 new divergences and 0 crashes, GLOOM 300-tick hashes held (workers 0 and 8) and 600-tick held, timedemo 1746772898. | 118d.2 | **An environment binding is guarded at run time ahead of a hoisted read; the block admits no binding that is a reference to a property or an element (fix; Astra's reload case at a9d7660a, 2026-09-11 20:23).** 118d.1 excused an environment name the unit declares as a value from the snapshot ahead of a quiet hoisted read, on the parser's annotation (`names_known_value`); a host may redeclare that name after the function compiled (`var& P = box.values;`), and the compiled read then met the getter after the inner read (the interpreter reads P first). An environment-name container ahead of a quiet inner read now takes the guard's plain-cell form (`op_operand_temp` c = 1): its executor resolves the binding once through the callee ladder and, when that resolution ran a getter (a property reference, a bare C++ property), leaves the value in the temp for the readers, else leaves null and the readers resolve the binding themselves; an environment-name left operand ahead of any hoisted read or in-op subscript takes a value temp. Inside a block the guard emits no copy (a site is a plain node once admitted; `cell_loop::env_guard_temps`, nulled by the flush), and the block's entry, its site resolution (loads and stores) and its landing refuse an environment binding that is a reference to a property or an element, read from the lookup cache without dereferencing it, so a getter-backed global runs its getter on every read. Controls (suite Cell Loops, 1 new): Astra's reload shape for a container and for a scalar (`caught|5 / caught|100`: the getter first after the redeclaration), a getter-backed container and scalar inside a loop (four getter runs for four reads on both backends), the listing of an environment container ahead of a quiet inner read closing as a block with the placeholder guard only. | this session (finding: Astra, backlog drain 2026-09-11) | Debug 3098x2 (interpreter and vm) at this commit; the chain's Release suites, fuzz chains, hashes, XTHREAD canary and census follow. | 118d.3 | **A guard temp is a value at its own point: an environment-name container takes the guard's always form, the plain-cell form is a slot form (fix; Astra's two defects in 118d.2 at e5b8c649, 2026-09-11 21:02, and the conservative proposal `always-env-guard.patch`).** 118d.2's plain-cell guard over an environment name represented "no snapshot" by a null temp, so a getter's captured null read as absence and the getter ran twice (the interpreter caught once), and it nulled every environment guard temp on the flush, a slot later statements reuse, so a live replay operand of the next statement was erased (`Integer overflow in '*'` became `Invalid operands for * operator`; a valid float-to-int conversion raised). Both are one representation rule: snapshot presence is not a value, and a physical temp slot is not one logical definition for the whole loop. An environment-name container ahead of any inner read now takes the always form (`op_operand_temp` c = 2) in both hoisting paths, at its own point, inside the block as its `C_GUARD` copy; the plain-cell form stays a slot form; the null protocol and the flush-time clearing are gone. The block's guard checks its temp first, so the steady state costs a slot read and a compare (as the plain-cell form did) and the source is resolved only when the temp does not hold the site's node. Controls (suite Cell Loops, 1 new): Astra's null-once getter (a redeclared property reference returning null, or null once then a table: `caught|1`, the getter runs once and its null is the container), the live-temp overflow (`Integer overflow in '*'|6|9223372036854775804`) and the live-temp conversion (`6|5`) on both backends with proofs on and off; the earlier listing control expects the guard and the counted loop's placeholder. | this session (finding: Astra, backlog drain 2026-09-11) | Debug 3099x2 (interpreter and vm) at this commit; the chain's Release suites, fuzz chains, hashes, XTHREAD canary and census follow. | 123 | **Program-scope declarations shadow by unit, as nested blocks shadow in C++ (Dev's ruling 2026-09-12; `docs/global_shadowing.md`).** Every source the engine executes is a unit (a file by its path, so a reload is the same unit; a literal by its content, so a hook string run every frame is one unit; a `jaibite` object by itself), numbered at first execution, the host being unit 0. A program-scope variable declaration by a unit that already declared the name writes its cell in place, as before; a declaration by another unit gets a new cell and the earlier cell stays the binding of the units numbered before it; function and class definitions replace in place whatever the unit (the hot-reload contract). Code resolves a name to its own unit's cell when that unit declared it, else to the cell declared most recently among those declared no later than the code's compile time (a stamp is the unit id and an execution number: top-level code carries its current run, so a reader string executed again reads as of now, which the sol2 and Squirrel benchmark constructors rely on when they re-execute `sum;` after each precompiled script; a function carries the run that minted it; a cell keeps the number of its first declaration when its unit rewrites it in place), else the newest cell (a forward reference keeps its late binding); code belongs to the unit that ran its declaration: `script_defined_function::unit` (named functions, lambdas, the VM's function protos at their run-time define, parallel copies), `function_decl::unit` on the AST (stamped by both class overload registrars, by a namespace's function registration and by a coroutine declaration, since methods, constructors, statics, coroutines and namespace functions build their function objects from the decl at call time) and `class_definition::unit` (field initializers). The running unit is ambient engine state, `string_symbolizer::current_unit` beside `env_epoch`: `engine::execute_parsed` sets it for a run and restores it after (nested executes from host callbacks nest), both backends set it from the callee at every call and restore it at the return (the VM's four record push paths, the tail splice, the fiber resume and the native-entry `call_script_function`; the interpreter's `call_function`) and around a class's field initializers; a worker (`parallel_worker_`, VM or interpreter) never writes it, its environments carry the private epoch sink whose shadow count stays zero. The root environment versions a binding in `define_variable` (the same unit as the flat cell, or a version with that unit, writes in place; another unit moves the flat cell to the per-name version list, takes the flat slot, notes the shadow and bumps the environment epoch, which re-arms every VM lookup site) and every lookup entry (`get_value_ptr`, `get_ref`, `get`, both `assign`s, `vm_storage_lookup`, `get_env_var_ptr`, `get_own_value_ptr`) consults `shadowed_cell` only when `shadow_count` is nonzero: one load and one predictable branch per environment lookup in a program that never shadows, nothing on the VM's armed head, and no unit in the lookup cache's key, since a chunk belongs to one unit. `::name` skips locals and members and resolves the same way; the host reads and assigns the newest cell (`add_global` writes it; a read outside any execution returns it); a host callback resolves as the running code does; a reference bound before a redeclaration stays on its cell. Three findings fixed on the way: the VM's general store resolved its environment target through `get_env_var_ptr` (stores landed on the newest cell while reads took the unit's); worker interpreters wrote the shared ambient unit through `call_function` (the chunk wrapper's function object is unit 0; interleaved saves and restores left 0 behind after a region, so the next declaration of the same program versioned instead of writing in place); and the interpreter's continuation-replay resume of a coroutine ran the body under the resumer's stamp (only the first resume went through `call_function`), so `resume_coroutine` now scopes the body's unit on every resume as the VM's fiber resume does. Controls (suite `Global Shadowing`, 8 rows, both backends, proofs on and off): code compiled before a redeclaration keeps its binding and code after sees the new one (writes through the old binding, the host's view, a third unit); the same unit redeclares in place and a forward reference keeps late binding (`extern var G;`); compound stores and increments through the old binding; a running loop keeps its cell across a host callback's redeclaration (and a later text sees the new cell); `::G` and the host API; classes (field initializer, method, `::G` in a method, static method), lambdas and coroutines resolve in their declaring unit; a reference bound before the redeclaration pins the old cell; a file reloaded at its path writes in place. Restated contract tests: coroutine resume across executes (the body keeps its unit's `g`, a same-path reload writes in place), dynamic import (the same reader text executed again reads module B), the module-scope closure probe (another text shadows, the same text re-executed writes in place), the sticky method environment (a same-path reload reads through, another unit shadows), and rows 119e.1, 118d.2 and 118d.3, which spell the in-place case through `execute_file_source` at one path (119e.1 keeps both: the same-unit retag lands by the new type, another unit's redeclaration leaves the loop on its own cell). | 114b | **The copy's container guard restores its own site's snapshot (a fix for row 114, found by Astra's review).** Two pinned table reads in different statements share one guard-temp slot (temps are reused per statement); row 114's entry wrote both tables' handles into that slot (the last one won) and the copy's guard only cleared a dead temp cell's dirty bit, so a bounds or kind miss at the first read resumed the general `T_LOAD` on the second table's handle (Astra's probes `guard_slot_reuse` and `guard_slot_reuse_kind`: 635 instead of the bounds error, a value instead of the declaration's rejection, on the VM with proofs on). The copy's guard (`op_c_guard`) now carries the copy offset of the `T_LOAD` it guards; when the guarded reference is a pinned table entry and the guard slot does not hold that site's node, it writes the reference's referent into the slot, exactly the general guard's snapshot, so the general form after any later miss reads the handle it expects; the entry no longer writes the guard slot. Controls (suite `Cell Loops`, 1 new): Astra's two probes land the same on both backends with proofs on and off (`caught:Array index 2 out of bounds for array of size 2`, the declaration's text); the frozen row 114 runner returns 635 on the bounds probe.
